WHAM-XREX-PFMG474 Firmware Changelog
=====================================

2026-09-23  —  Review of the bundled CONFig:*/telemetry/host-tooling
                work: 6 real bugs found and fixed
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Autonomous follow-up (direct authorization), a genuine
        independent review of the work that rode along with the
        PID:->SOURce:/SHOT:/LOG:/CHANnel: rename but never got its own
        pass (the rename itself already got an 8-angle review, see the
        prior entry). Three review passes -- pid.c/xrex_io.c's
        CONFig:* additions, telemetry.c, and the ~2700 combined new
        lines in wham_console.py/run_simulator_validation.py -- found
        6 real, concrete bugs, none of them hypothetical.

        FIRMWARE (Core/Src/pid.c, Core/Src/main.c):

        1. Four call sites in pid.c still read the compile-time
           PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ macro instead of the runtime
           g_pidLoopRateHz variable CONFig:PIDRate (2026-09-22) actually
           changes: PID_GetLogSampleRateHz() (LOG:DATA?'s own reported
           sample rate), PID_StartRamp() (SOURce:RAMP's duration),
           PID_SetProfileTiming()/PID_GetProfileTiming() (SHOT:TIMing/
           SHOT:TIMing?). CONFig:PIDRate itself worked correctly and
           reported success -- these four commands silently kept using
           the stale 1000 Hz default regardless, so a shot's real
           ramp/flat-top/ramp-down durations ran 2x too fast or slow
           after a loop-rate change, invisibly (SHOT:TIMing? read back
           exactly what was asked for, using the same stale constant).
           All four fixed to read g_pidLoopRateHz. A narrower, deeper
           issue flagged but NOT fixed: SHOT:TIMing's stored tick
           counts are computed once at set-time and go stale again if
           CONFig:PIDRate changes a second time before the shot
           actually fires (allowed -- PID_SetLoopRateHz() only refuses
           while g_running != 0) -- a real gap, but a different design
           question (re-derive ticks at fire time?) than this bug.

        2. Minor, related: PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ_MIN was 500 Hz, below the
           ~649 Hz real HRTIM hardware floor its own comment cited --
           not unsafe (fails closed, HRTIM1_SetPidHeartbeatRate()
           correctly rejects the whole [500,648] sub-range), just
           presented a dead range as valid with no way to tell from the
           bound alone. Raised to 700 Hz; docs/command_reference.md
           updated to match.

        3. Telemetry_Init() (main.c) ran AFTER the boot-time fault poll
           (SM_Init()/SM_PollFaults()/XrexIo_Poll*Faults()), which
           exists specifically to catch a fault already latched at
           power-on. When that happened, EnterFault() correctly pushed
           the event into both the live !EVT ring and the flight
           recorder -- and Telemetry_Init(), running right after,
           unconditionally zeroed both, permanently erasing the one
           event SYS:EVLOG? exists to survive for post-mortem review
           (no later call ever re-pushes it -- every fault-report path
           dedupes on already being in SM_STATE_FAULT). Moved
           Telemetry_Init() before the fault-poll block.

        HOST TOOLING (python/run_simulator_validation.py):

        4. generate_combined_plot() referenced `survivor_rows`, not an
           actual parameter or local (the real name is `extra_traces`)
           -- a NameError on any scenario whose controller log came
           back genuinely empty (a legitimate `OK 0 ...` reply, not an
           error), which discarded that scenario's own already-computed
           real PASS/FAIL verdict and replaced it with a generic
           "Unexpected exception" in the campaign report. Fixed to
           check `extra_traces`.

        5. Several scenarios (OCP/Enerpro/Watertemp fault injection,
           ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT-dropped, plus two more for consistency)
           armed the simulator's onboard log with a hardcoded
           min_interval_ms=5 -- only 5.0s of coverage (1000 samples *
           5ms) -- instead of this file's own sim_log_interval_ms(),
           added earlier specifically to fix this exact bug class
           elsewhere. With the module default RAMP_S=10.0/FLAT_S=5.0,
           the fault-injection scenarios fire their fault at ~10.5s --
           well past the 5.0s cutoff -- silently truncating the
           simulator-side DRIVE/FEEDBACK log before the fault instant,
           corrupting the diagnostic plot/CSV for exactly the tests
           meant to visually confirm hardware fault response (did not
           affect PASS/FAIL, which polls STATE?/SOURce:STATus?
           directly, not this log). All 7 real call sites now use
           sim_log_interval_ms() consistently.

        6. shot_meta() built its returned dict with the pre-rename key
           `ramp_time_s`, but wham_console.py's generate_plot()/
           generate_multi_channel_plot() were updated in the same
           rename to require separate ramp_up_time_s/ramp_down_time_s
           (once SHOT:TIMing became a 3-argument asymmetric-ramp
           command) -- shot_meta() was never updated to match, so
           have_profile was always False and every plot this script
           produced silently fell back to the plain Hz-only view
           instead of the intended phase-shaded ramp/flat-top/ramp-down
           + Amps-axis plot, for essentially the whole campaign, no
           error printed. A second consumer of the same stale key
           inside this same file (generate_multi_channel_grid_plot()'s
           own phase-shading) was also broken the same way and also
           fixed. This script only ever commands symmetric ramps, so
           both new keys are set to the same value.

        Verified: wham_build.py --target controller/--target simulator
        --clean both clean (zero errors/warnings). Python: py_compile +
        real imports clean; shot_meta()'s new keys and the resulting
        have_profile condition functionally tested True; confirmed zero
        remaining hardcoded min_interval_ms=5 call sites (only the
        function's own default parameter, which is fine, remains).
        Firmware changes not yet reflashed/hardware-verified as of this
        entry -- next step.

2026-09-23  —  ARM-readiness stub: investigated, comments corrected,
                deliberately NOT closed further
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Autonomous follow-up (direct authorization) on a known,
        long-flagged gap: ArmConditionsMet() (state_machine.c) still
        carried a "TODO, populate the rest" comment claiming profile
        timing / gains sanity / "whatever else turns out to matter"
        were unchecked, even though the function itself already checks
        two real conditions (the external-enable interlock, and every
        enabled channel's ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT readiness).

        Investigated each item the stale comment claimed was missing:
        "profile timing configured" turns out NOT to be a real gap --
        PID_ProfileStart() (pid.c, called from SM_Fire()/SHOT:STARt)
        already independently refuses to fire with no timing set, so
        ARM succeeding without it is harmless (ARM changes nothing
        electrical either way). "No fault active" was never a separate
        check to add -- IDLE and FAULT are already mutually exclusive
        states, covered for free by SM_Arm()'s own state check.

        Deliberately did NOT add a gain-sanity check: there is no
        universal "wrong" PID gain value to enforce without real domain
        input on what's actually unsafe for this specific plant --
        guessing a bound here would be worse than not checking at all,
        and this is exactly the kind of safety-relevant behavior change
        that shouldn't be made unilaterally while unattended. Flagged
        clearly in the corrected comments instead of guessed at.

        Corrected the stale "currently a stub"/"always allows arming"/
        "TODO, populate the rest" comments in both state_machine.c
        (ArmConditionsMet() itself, header and closing comment) and
        state_machine.h (the ARMED state's own design writeup, and
        SM_Arm()'s declaration comment) to accurately describe what's
        actually checked today. state_machine.h's own "ALSO NOTE TO
        REVISIT, PARTIALLY RESOLVED" paragraph was already accurate --
        left unchanged.

        Comment-only change, no behavior change. Verified: wham_build.py
        --target controller --clean compiles clean (zero errors/
        warnings) -- not reflashed, since nothing electrical changed.

2026-09-23  —  Closed the two remaining PID-loop-rate/log-size
                staleness gaps (host tooling only)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Autonomous follow-up (direct authorization) to two gaps
        flagged but left open in earlier entries.

        1. python/wham_console.py's sync_calibration() now also
           queries CONFig:PIDRate? and refreshes
           PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ_ASSUMED, same live-sync treatment already
           applied to the Amps<->Hz globals -- wired into the same two
           call sites (connect-time, and default()'s post-command
           resync via _is_calibration_set(), now also matching
           CONFig:PIDRate). python/run_simulator_validation.py's own
           hardcoded PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ = 1000 copy removed;
           pid_log_decim() now reads wc.PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ_ASSUMED
           directly (module-qualified, tracks live syncs -- same
           pattern already used for wc.PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A).

        2. *** REAL BUG, FOUND while fixing (1), not what was being
           looked for ***: wham_console.py's PID_LOG_MAX_SAMPLES_ASSUMED
           was hardcoded 1000. Core/Inc/pid.h's real PID_LOG_MAX_SAMPLES
           is 1750, and always has been -- confirmed directly against
           source. This one isn't a live-sync gap at all (no CONFig:*
           command backs a compile-time log-buffer size); it was just
           wrong, unconditionally, underestimating real log capacity by
           43% in the shot wizard's auto-suggested decim value. Fixed
           to the correct constant.

        3. python/dslogic_shot_capture.py's own third independent
           hardcoded copy of PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ/PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ/
           PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A -- NOT simply merged with wham_console.py's
           copy: that module's own header comment says it's deliberately
           kept as a separate copy so it stays usable standalone,
           without wham_console.py's heavier dependencies -- and
           wham_console.py already imports dslogic_shot_capture.py at
           its own top level, so having dslogic_shot_capture.py import
           wham_console back would risk a real circular-import problem
           depending on which script runs as __main__. Added
           dslogic_shot_capture.set_calibration(turnon_hz, max_hz,
           max_current_a) instead -- no device query, no wham_console
           import -- and wired wham_console.py's do_shot() DSLogic
           integration to push its own already-fetched live values
           through after every successful sync_calibration(), at both
           call sites (connect-time and the raw-command resync path).
           Standalone use of dslogic_shot_capture.py is completely
           unaffected -- its hardcoded defaults are untouched, this only
           adds an optional live-refresh path for callers that have one.

        Verified: all three files syntax-check and import cleanly
        (py_compile + real imports, confirmed no circular-import issue
        in practice -- wham_console imports fine with
        dslogic_shot_capture already imported into it, and vice versa
        standalone); dslogic_shot_capture.set_calibration() functionally
        tested (updates its module globals correctly);
        run_simulator_validation.pid_log_decim(10.0) returns 8,
        matching ceil(1000*10*1.25/1750). No firmware changes --
        python/ only.

2026-09-22  —  Rename review: 5 findings fixed, build-verified before
                first flash
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  The PID: -> SOURce:/SHOT:/LOG:/CHANnel: rename (next entry
        below) was hand-edited directly across cmd_parser.c/commands.c/
        commands.h/state_machine.h -- never compiled or flashed before
        this pass. An 8-angle review (correctness/removed-behavior/
        cross-file/reuse/simplification/efficiency/altitude/
        conventions) confirmed the mechanical rename itself was clean
        (zero broken dispatch, zero orphaned old-namespace strings
        anywhere in Core/, the Python tooling, or the docs -- every
        SendErr()'s argument-count/range logic and every state-machine
        safety guarantee carried over verbatim) but found 5 real issues
        in code that rode along in the same hand-editing pass:

        1. commands.h's ERR 13/15 doc table only listed ARM/DISARM/
           SHOT:STARt as sources -- FIRE and SOURce:RUN (and ARM itself,
           for ERR 15) also emit these codes now that the ARM-state gate
           was extended to them. Fixed: table updated to name all the
           real sources and explain why it widened.
        2. The ARM-state + external-enable-interlock precondition check
           (SM_GetState()==ARMED, SM_ExternalEnableOk()) was duplicated
           byte-for-byte in cmd_fire/cmd_source_run/cmd_shot_start --
           three independent copies of safety-interlock logic, one easy
           to miss during a future edit. Fixed: factored into a new
           static RequireArmedAndEnabled(inst) helper (commands.c,
           right before cmd_fire) all three now call; cmd_arm()'s own
           similar-but-distinct check (different message, a third
           precondition) deliberately stays separate, not folded in.
        3-4. Two stale doc-comment references left by a DIFFERENT,
           already-completed change bundled into the same session (the
           emergency-stop feature removal) -- state_machine.h still
           named the removed SM_FAULT_EMERGENCY_STOP as a live sibling
           of SM_FAULT_EXTERNAL_ENABLE, and commands.h still listed the
           removed EMERGency:INPut? command as a live raw-readback
           command (the identical comment in xrex_io.h had already been
           correctly updated -- only this twin copy was missed). Both
           fixed.
        5. SM_Fire()/SM_StartPlain() (state_machine.c) are near-
           duplicate bodies -- left as-is: the author's own comment
           already documents this as a deliberate tradeoff (a mode-flag
           on SM_Fire() was rejected in favor of a separate entry
           point), not an oversight.

        Verified after fixing: wham_build.py --target controller --clean
        and --target simulator --clean both clean (zero errors,
        zero warnings) -- the FIRST successful build of this rename.
        python/wham_console.py and python/run_simulator_validation.py
        both syntax-check and import cleanly (py_compile + a real
        import, not just a grep).

        Process note for next time: this rename was interleaved,
        hunk-by-hunk, with ~8 unrelated feature additions in the same
        files (CONFig:*, telemetry, DEBUG:FAULT:BYPASS, the E-stop
        removal) -- no isolated "rename only" diff ever existed to
        review in isolation, which is exactly what made this pass take
        8 independent angles instead of one straightforward read. A
        project-wide case-insensitive grep for every retired mnemonic/
        function name, run as its own explicit step immediately after a
        rename (before moving on to other edits in the same files), is
        cheap insurance against this class of drift -- worth making a
        habit rather than relying on a later audit to catch it.

2026-09-22  —  Wire-protocol rename: PID: -> SOURce:/SHOT:/LOG:/CHANnel:
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: rename the output/demand SCPI commands out of
        the PID: namespace into clean top-level namespaces, leaving PID:
        to mean only the genuine PID-loop parameters. Clean cut, no
        aliases -- the old PID:START/PID:SETPOINT/... spellings now
        fall through to ERR 1 (unknown command), never silently
        accepted.

        Old -> new (canonical spellings; matching is case-insensitive):
          PID:START            -> SOURce:RUN
          PID:STOP             -> SOURce:STOP
          PID:SETPOINT         -> SOURce:SETpoint
          PID:STATus?          -> SOURce:STATus?
          PID:RAMP             -> SOURce:RAMP
          PID:CHANnel:ENAble   -> SOURce:ENAble
          PID:CHANnel:ENAble?  -> SOURce:ENAble?
          PID:CHANnel:NICKname -> CHANnel:NICKname
          PID:CHANnel:NICKname? -> CHANnel:NICKname?
          PID:PROFile:TIMing   -> SHOT:TIMing
          PID:PROFile:TIMing?  -> SHOT:TIMing?
          PID:PROFile:CURRent  -> SHOT:CURRent
          PID:PROFile:CURRent? -> SHOT:CURRent?
          PID:PROFile:STARt    -> SHOT:STARt
          PID:LOG              -> LOG:ARM
          PID:LOGDATA?         -> LOG:DATA?

        PID:GAINS/PID:GAINS?/PID:LOOPMODE/PID:LOOPMODE? are unchanged --
        they stay under PID:, which now means only those four. Error
        strings updated too: "Invalid PID channel" -> "Invalid channel"
        and "Invalid PID:* argument" -> "Invalid command arguments"
        (ERR 11/12).

        The underlying pid.c API, all units, and every command's reply
        shape are unchanged -- a wire-protocol rename only, no behavior,
        tuning, or calibration change. TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_VERSION bumped
        3 -> 4 to mark the wire change.

        Updated in lockstep: python/wham_console.py,
        python/wham_llm_console.py, python/run_simulator_validation.py,
        python/dslogic_shot_capture.py, and the docs
        (docs/command_reference.md, docs/sop/wham_xrex_pfmg474_sop.tex,
        docs/telemetry.md, AGENTS.md, docs/pin_mapping_reference.tex).

2026-09-22  —  Docs: SOP Rev D (telemetry section, ARM/trigger update),
                command_reference.md gaps filled
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: "Update the SOP and command manifest for new
        commands and functionality. In the SOP, include a section about
        telemetry and translation."

        docs/command_reference.md: audited the full command_table[]
        (cmd_parser.c, ~90 real entries) against the doc and closed the
        two genuine gaps found -- DEBUG:FAULT:BYPASS/? (the bench-only
        all-fault-types bypass) and DIAGnostic:OPTBytes? (the option-
        byte boot-config diagnostic) were both implemented but entirely
        undocumented. Also resolved a now-stale "known limitation" note
        claiming wham_console.py's Amps<->Hz calibration constants were
        hardcoded with no live sync -- that was fixed earlier this same
        day (sync_calibration()); the note now describes the fix
        instead of the gap it used to warn about.

        docs/sop/wham_xrex_pfmg474_sop.tex, Rev D:
        - New Section 10, "Telemetry -- Reading and Translating the
          Event Stream": the live !EVT stream (SYS:EVENT), a translation
          table mapping every !EVT line shape to its operational
          meaning (STATE/TRIGGER FIRING/FAULT/FAULT CLEAR -- including
          how TRIGGER FIRING is the only way to tell a trigger-initiated
          shot apart from a command-initiated one), and the flight
          recorder (SYS:EVLOG?) for post-mortem review after the live
          stream wasn't being watched. Includes a worked example reusing
          a real incident from earlier this same day (a spontaneous
          FAULT GENERAL, diagnosed via SYS:EVLOG? rather than guessing).
        - Section 9 updated for two real Rev D firmware behavior changes
          that directly affect this procedure: EXTernal:ENAble/
          EXTernal:TRIGger now default ON (previously opt-in/OFF), which
          can newly block Step 7's ARM on hardware with nothing wired to
          PF13 depending on how that board's pin rests; and ARM's
          failure reply now names the specific precondition that failed
          instead of one generic error. Documented firing via the
          external trigger as an alternative to Step 8's manual
          PID:PROFile:STARt (Step 8a/8b), with its own CAUTION addendum:
          an ARMED board with the trigger on can fire at any later
          moment, not just immediately, so the safety check belongs
          before ARM in this mode, not just before the trigger pull.
        - Troubleshooting table: updated the ARM entry for the new
          specific error messages, added a row for the new
          EXTernal:ENAble default surprising an unwired bench setup, and
          a row for "the external trigger doesn't fire anything, no
          error reported" (a triggered fire fails silently by design --
          this is the exact failure mode debugged live earlier the same
          day, root cause: profile timing wiped by a reflash).
        - *** REAL BUG, FOUND AND FIXED same day, confirmed via PDF
          render ***: the Troubleshooting table used a plain
          tabular/center (can't break across a page), and the two new
          rows pushed it past one page -- pdflatex reported an "Overfull
          vbox (392pt too high)", which rendered as a blank page
          followed by the table's last row cut off entirely, invisible
          to a reader unless someone actually looked at the compiled
          PDF rather than just the source. Fixed by switching to
          longtable (already imported in the preamble, unused until
          now) with a repeating header row; re-rendered and visually
          confirmed clean across both continuation pages.
        - Added a docs/telemetry.md reference; Rev D revision-table row.

        Verified: docs/command_reference.md changes reviewed for
        accuracy against the actual source (xrex_io.c/state_machine.c/
        commands.c); SOP compiled twice with pdflatex (stable page
        count and cross-references), then every changed/added page
        inspected visually via PDF render -- title/revision table,
        Section 9's reworked ARM step and new Step 8a/8b, Section 10 in
        full, the fixed Troubleshooting table across its page break, and
        References. No firmware changes -- docs only.

2026-09-22  —  ARM now reports WHY it refused, not just that it did
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: "when we attempt to make a state change, can
        we have the controller report why it could not move to the next
        state if relevant? For instance, when I go to ARM the
        controller, if we don't have a specific output track correctly
        programmed, we should report this." cmd_arm() (commands.c) used
        to collapse every failure reason -- wrong state, the
        external-enable interlock, a specific channel's ENA_OUT/
        CONTACT_OUT not both set -- into one generic
        ERR 13 "not currently IDLE, or arm conditions not met". An
        operator with one misconfigured channel out of four had no way
        to tell which one from the wire protocol alone.

        cmd_arm() now checks each precondition explicitly, in the same
        order ArmConditionsMet() (state_machine.c) already checks them
        internally, and reports the FIRST failing one distinctly --
        matching the "distinct failure reasons, reported distinctly"
        pattern cmd_pid_profile_start()/cmd_pid_start() already
        established for PID:PROFile:STARt/PID:START (2026-09-16/
        2026-09-21):
          ERR 13  Can't ARM -- not currently IDLE
          ERR 15  External enable interlock not satisfied -- PF13 reads LOW
          ERR 13  Channel N's ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT aren't both set -- see
                  XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut, or PID:CHANnel:ENAble 0
                  if unused

        New Core/Src/xrex_io.c function XrexIo_FindNotReadyChannel() --
        same per-channel scan XrexIo_EnableOutputsReadyToArm() already
        did (now implemented in terms of this function instead of
        duplicating the loop), but returns the first offending 0-based
        channel instead of collapsing to a bool; 0xFF means every
        enabled channel is ready, matching SM_GetFaultChannel()'s own
        established not-applicable sentinel convention.

        SM_Arm() itself (state_machine.c) is unchanged -- its own
        internal ArmConditionsMet() check still backs the actual
        transition as a last-line-of-defense against a narrow race
        window between cmd_arm()'s new checks and the SM_Arm() call
        (same reasoning as SM_Fire()'s own re-check of
        SM_ExternalEnableOk()), falling back to a generic message that
        should never fire outside that race.

        docs/command_reference.md's ARM section and the ERR 15 table
        row updated to match (ERR 15 is no longer PID:PROFile:STARt-only
        -- ARM can now generate it too).

        *** REAL BUG, FOUND AND FIXED same day, confirmed on real
        hardware ***: the channel-not-ready message's first cut used
        char buf[64] in cmd_arm() -- the full text ("Channel N's
        ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT aren't both set -- see
        XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut, or PID:CHANnel:ENAble 0 if
        unused") is 120 chars, so snprintf silently truncated it
        mid-word ("...see XREX:CHA") -- null-terminated within the
        given size rather than overflowing, so nothing crashed or
        corrupted, it just quietly sent a cut-off error message. Caught
        immediately by live-testing the new message on real hardware
        rather than just reading the code. Fixed by growing the buffer
        to 96 bytes AND shortening the message itself (dropped the
        trailing "or PID:CHANnel:ENAble 0 if unused" clause) -- the
        core "which channel, which check" information is what actually
        matters. docs/command_reference.md's example updated to match
        the shorter text.

        Verified on real hardware, all three failure paths: wrong state
        (ARM while already ARMED), and a specific channel's output not
        ready (fresh-boot default: channel enabled, ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT
        never asserted) -- both report their full, untruncated messages
        and the underlying transitions behave correctly (DISARM back to
        IDLE, a properly-configured channel ARMs cleanly). The external-
        enable path (ERR 15) shares the exact same SM_ExternalEnableOk()
        check already proven working via PID:PROFile:STARt's identical
        ERR 15 earlier this session -- not independently re-tested here,
        since it needs PF13 physically driven LOW to exercise (this
        board's own fiber receiver idles HIGH with nothing wired to it).

2026-09-22  —  External trigger reports itself over telemetry:
               "!EVT ... TRIGGER FIRING"
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  When the external trigger (rising edge on PF15 while ARMED)
        successfully fires a shot, emit a dedicated telemetry event
        "!EVT <tick> TRIGGER FIRING" in addition to the existing
        "!EVT ... STATE FIRING" (so a host can distinguish a
        trigger-initiated shot from a command-initiated one).

        - telemetry.c/h: new TELEM_EVT_TRIGGER event kind +
          Telemetry_PushTrigger(), rendered "!EVT <tick> TRIGGER FIRING".
          TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_VERSION -> 3 (new !EVT payload type).
        - state_machine.c (SM_PollFaults, external-trigger branch): now
          checks SM_Fire()'s return and pushes the trigger event only when
          the shot actually fired -- a trigger while not-ready / profile
          timing unset stays silent, exactly like a failed PID:PROFILE:START.

        Verified: clean build (both targets), flashed to the controller
        (SYS:TELEM? -> OK 3, EXTernal:TRIGger? -> OK 1). NOT exercised live
        end-to-end: PF15 is wired to the physical trigger source, not the
        PD1/DIAGnostic:GPOut12 loopback, so a software-driven rising edge
        could not be injected from the console -- needs a physical trigger
        (or the loopback restored) to observe the TRIGGER FIRING line.

2026-09-22  —  EXTernal:ENAble/EXTernal:TRIGger now ON by default
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct instruction: "we should leave the external enable and
        trigger on by default -- that is, these are usable features by
        default." g_externalEnableRequired and g_externalTriggerRequired
        (state_machine.c) both flip from 0U to 1U at declaration -- the
        only change; SM_SetExternalEnableRequired()/
        SM_SetExternalTriggerRequired() and every check/gating site
        (ArmConditionsMet(), SM_Fire(), the FIRING-time fault check,
        FAULT:CLEAR's re-validation, the PF15 edge-fire path) are
        unmodified, since this is purely a startup-default change, not a
        behavior change. Both remain RAM-only config (like every other
        runtime setting in this project) -- reset to ON on every reboot,
        not persisted; `EXTernal:ENAble 0`/`EXTernal:TRIGger 0` opt back
        out for a session same as before.

        Real, not paper, consequence for EXTernal:ENAble specifically:
        PF13 is GPIO_PULLDOWN (main.c), so a floating/unwired PF13 now
        reads LOW = "not satisfied" out of the box -- ARM and
        PID:PROFile:STARt will both refuse (ERR 13/ERR 15) on any bench
        setup that doesn't have the real interlock wired, looped back
        (DIAGnostic:GPOut11 -> PF13), or EXTernal:ENAble 0 sent first.
        This affects every raw-SCPI/shot-wizard session from here on,
        including this project's own docs/sop/wham_xrex_pfmg474_sop.tex
        Section 9 procedure (not updated -- it doesn't mention
        EXTernal:ENAble either way, so no correction needed there, but
        an operator hitting a NEW ERR 13 following that procedure on
        unwired hardware should check EXTernal:ENAble? first).
        EXTernal:TRIGger's default has much lower practical impact: it
        only means a PF15 rising edge can fire an already-ARMED shot,
        never blocks anything by itself, and a floating/unwired PF15
        (also GPIO_PULLDOWN) simply never produces a rising edge -- an
        unwired bench setup sees no behavior change from that flag
        alone.

        python/run_simulator_validation.py's automated campaign is
        UNAFFECTED: reset_to_safe_state() already sent
        `EXTernal:ENAble 0` explicitly before/after every scenario
        (needed for its software-only ARM/FIRE design, since that rig
        has nothing wired to PF13/PF15 either) -- updated its own
        top-of-file comment and reset_to_safe_state()'s doc comment to
        stop describing the old default as current, but no functional
        change was needed there.

        Documentation updated to match: state_machine.c's own
        g_externalEnableRequired/g_externalTriggerRequired comments,
        state_machine.h's SM_GetExternalEnableRequired()/the
        external-trigger design comment ("Opt-in, OFF by default"),
        docs/command_reference.md's EXTernal:ENAble and EXTernal:TRIGger
        sections (both previously said "Opt-in, OFF by default").

        Verified: wham_build.py --target controller --clean and
        --target simulator --clean both clean (zero errors/warnings).

2026-09-22  —  Telemetry Phase 4: flight recorder + SYS:EVLOG? (post-mortem
               event history); console `evlog` command
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  docs/telemetry.md Phase 4. New retained event history alongside
        the existing live !EVT stream:

        - telemetry.c: a 128-entry flight-recorder ring (overwrite-oldest)
          that every pushed event is ALSO recorded to -- separate from the
          transmit queue, so it is never consumed by emission. The !EVT
          line renderer was factored into EmitEventLine(), shared by the
          live stream (Telemetry_PollEmit) and the replay
          (Telemetry_ReplayFlightLog) so both render identically.
        - SYS:EVLOG? (commands.c/cmd_parser.c): replays the retained
          history oldest-first as "OK <n>" followed by n one-line !EVT
          packets. Always recorded -- SYS:EVENT 0 silences the live stream
          without losing the post-mortem log. RAM-only (clears on reset);
          .noinit warm-reset retention is a later step.
        - TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_VERSION -> 2 (new command).
        - wham_console.py: query_evlog() (multi-line read via _read_line())
          + `evlog` console command.

        Verified on real hardware (controller, freshly flashed): three
        consecutive SYS:EVLOG? queries each returned OK 6 + six clean
        !EVT lines in order (ARM/FIRING/FAULT GENERAL/FAULT CLEAR/ARMED/
        IDLE); SYS:EVENT 0 gated the live stream while the recorder kept
        recording (history grew from 4 to 6 across the gated period).
        A host-side !EVT/reply desync (a stray "!" gluing onto the next
        reply as "!OK") was root-caused to WhamLink.query()'s
        reset_input_buffer() clipping an in-flight line, and fixed via
        _read_line() (separate entry below).

2026-09-22  —  wham_console.py: fixed a !EVT/reply desync race, live
                Amps<->Hz calibration sync (host-only, no firmware change)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Two host-tooling gaps found and fixed while live-verifying
        the eight new CONFig:* commands (previous entry, same day) on
        real hardware.

        1. WhamLink.query()/drain_events() used to call
           self.ser.reset_input_buffer() right before writing the next
           command, on the assumption that whatever was sitting in the
           RX buffer at that instant "could only be a stale, already-
           fully-arrived line." That assumption broke in practice:
           flipping CONFig:FaultPolarity:OCP live (with the OCP pin
           floating on the bench) trips a real per-channel OVERCURRENT
           fault, which emits its own !EVT line a poll cycle after the
           command's own OK reply -- real, observed latency. Firing the
           next command with ~no delay could catch that !EVT line still
           trickling in over the 115200-baud UART, and
           reset_input_buffer() silently ate the tail of it, desyncing
           every reply after it by one line (a bare "1" surfacing as one
           query's reply, the real "OK 1" landing on the NEXT query,
           etc. -- confirmed by reproducing it standalone before fixing
           it, then re-running the identical zero-delay sequence
           afterward with no further desync).

           Fixed by replacing the one-shot read+discard design with a
           persistent per-link byte buffer (WhamLink._rx_buf) and a new
           _read_line() that only ever consumes a COMPLETE line, leaving
           a still-arriving partial line buffered for the next call
           instead of discarding it. reset_input_buffer() is gone
           entirely -- drain_events() and query() now both go through
           _read_line(), so pre-command buffer hygiene can no longer
           clip an in-flight line. Verified by deliberately reproducing
           the original race (flip OCP polarity to trip a real fault,
           immediately follow with an 8-query rapid-fire burst, zero
           delay) three separate times post-fix -- every reply came back
           correctly framed.

        2. PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ/PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ/PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A
           (wham_console.py's Amps<->Hz plot-axis conversion constants)
           were hardcoded copies of ctrlr_config.h's compile-time
           defaults -- a known gap flagged in the previous entry, since
           CONFig:TURNONHz/MAXFREQHz/MAXCURRent now make the real values
           live-changeable. Added sync_calibration(link), which queries
           the three CONFig:*? commands and refreshes the module
           globals; wired into WhamConsole._on_connect() (automatic on
           every connect/reconnect) and default() via the new
           _is_calibration_set() (automatic re-sync right after a raw
           CONFig:TURNONHz/MAXFREQHz/MAXCURRent typed directly, the same
           way _is_profile_start() already triggers auto-plot after a
           raw PID:PROFile:STARt). Never raises -- an older firmware or
           any query error just leaves the last-known values in place,
           since this only feeds a plot axis, not a safety check.

           Also added amps_to_hz() (the missing inverse of the existing
           hz_to_amps()) and used it to replace four hand-inlined copies
           of the same formula in run_simulator_validation.py, one of
           which additionally hardcoded PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ as a bare
           100000.0 -- doubly stale, now fixed the same way. That file's
           `from wham_console import PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ, PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A`
           was also a live-sync trap in its own right: a `from` import
           freezes a copy at import time, so mutating wham_console's own
           globals later would never have reached it -- changed to
           `import wham_console as wc` with module-qualified access
           (wc.PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ etc.) everywhere those values are read.
           Two module-level constants derived from PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A at
           import time (MATRIX_DEMAND_LEVELS_A, GAIN_SMOOTHNESS_DEMAND_A)
           are now recomputed in main() right after connecting and
           syncing, same pattern this file already used for RAMP_S/
           FLAT_S; also caught and fixed a related bug this surfaced --
           run_gain_smoothness_sweep()'s demand_a=GAIN_SMOOTHNESS_
           DEMAND_A default argument would have stayed frozen at its
           pre-sync value even after the recompute (default arguments
           bind once at module load), fixed by resolving it inside the
           function body instead (demand_a=None, then read the current
           global if still None).

        PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ in run_simulator_validation.py has the exact
        same kind of gap now that CONFig:PIDRate exists (still a
        hand-kept-in-sync copy, used for PID:LOG/PID:LOGDATA? sample-
        rate math) -- flagged in that file's own comment, not fixed
        here (out of scope for this pass).

        Verified live against the real controller: sync_calibration()
        correctly picks up a live CONFig:TURNONHz change and
        amps_to_hz()/hz_to_amps() immediately reflect it; _is_
        calibration_set() unit-tested against every SCPI short/long-
        form variant and confirmed it does NOT fire for the `?` query
        forms or unrelated CONFig:* setters; WhamConsole's own
        connect flow prints the synced values. No firmware changes --
        python/ only.

2026-09-22  —  Eight ctrlr_config.h values made runtime-configurable
                (CONFig:* commands)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ, PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ,
        PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ, PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A_PER_CHANNEL,
        PID_OUTPUT_MAX_SLEW_HZ_PER_TICK, FAULT_RAMP_DOWN_TIME_S, the
        four fault-pin polarities, and PFM_MAX_CARRIER_FREQ_HZ were all
        compile-time-only -- changing any of them meant editing
        ctrlr_config.h, rebuilding, and reflashing. Each is now backed
        by a mutable variable + setter/getter pair and a matching
        CONFig:* SCPI command (see docs/command_reference.md), same
        session-only convention as PID:GAINS/PID:PROFile:TIMing (resets
        to the ctrlr_config.h default on every reboot/reflash).

        PID_LOOP_RATE_HZ is the one value that reaches real hardware
        state, not just a variable: CONFig:PIDRate live-reprograms the
        HRTIM Master timebase period register directly
        (HRTIM1_SetPidHeartbeatRate(), hrtim.c, writing
        sMasterRegs.MPER -- no HAL macro for this exists in this HAL,
        confirmed by grep) and PID_DT_SEC (used by every channel's
        integral/derivative math) is recomputed atomically alongside it
        so the two can never drift. It is also the one setter that
        refuses to change while a shot is running (ERR 12) -- unlike
        every other value here, changing the control loop's own sample
        rate mid-shot would corrupt every running channel's integral
        accumulator, slew-clamp history, and profile tick counting,
        which all assume a constant dt across the shot.

        PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ/PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ (the AmpsToHz() calibration
        endpoints) are cross-validated against each other (turnon
        strictly below max) and against PID_OUTPUT_MIN_HZ/MAX_HZ.
        PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A_PER_CHANNEL is settable per channel --  this
        is the actual per-channel calibration ctrlr_config.h's own
        "MUST BE CALIBRATED BEFORE FINAL DEPLOYMENT" comment refers to.
        The four fault polarities (XR_WATER/TMP/ENERPRO/OCP_FLT_
        POLARITY, xrex_io.c) got a shared SetFaultPolarity() helper
        behind four thin CONFig:FaultPolarity:WATER/TEMP/ENERPRO/OCP
        wrappers, placed outside the BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR guard so
        they compile/work on both targets even though only the
        controller's real fault-detection logic reads them.
        PFM_MAX_CARRIER_FREQ_HZ (the legacy TABle:STEP carrier ceiling)
        is now PFM_SetMaxCarrierFreqHz()/TableStepMinPer(), replacing
        the old TABLE_STEP_MIN_PER macro, with the same 16-bit HRTIM
        PER-register-fit check the macro used to get for free at
        compile time.

        Known gap, flagged but not addressed: python/wham_console.py
        and python/run_simulator_validation.py hardcode their own
        Python copies of PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ/PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ/
        PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A for host-side Amps<->Hz plot conversions.
        Changing the real values via these new commands does not
        update those Python constants -- an Amps-axis plotted after
        such a change will be silently wrong (the Hz-axis stays
        correct). Neither host tool queries these live yet.

        Hit the same "*/ inside a /* */ comment" bug twice while
        writing doc comments containing the literal text "PID:*/
        CONFig:*" (the */ prematurely closes the comment, corrupting
        following code into a syntax error) -- once in pid.h, once in
        commands.c, both caught by the build and fixed by rewording to
        "PID:* or CONFig:*". Added a proactive
        `grep -n 'PID:\*/\|:\*/[A-Za-z]'` check before every later
        build in this session to catch this class of bug before
        wasting a build cycle.

        Verified: wham_build.py --target controller --clean and
        --target simulator --clean both clean (zero errors/warnings)
        after all eight value groups were added -- pid.c/xrex_io.c/
        commands.c are shared between both targets.

2026-09-22  —  Telemetry: "one line per packet" protocol invariant +
               waveform log now captures the fault ramp-down
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Two pieces of docs/telemetry.md Phase 3, both driven by a live
        hardware demo of the telemetry stream.

        1. Formalized a hard wire-protocol invariant (docs/telemetry.md,
           "Packet framing invariant"): EVERY telemetry packet is exactly
           one CRLF-terminated line -- replies, !EVT events, and future
           stream/flight-recorder lines alike -- with no embedded CR/LF, so
           a host never needs length-prefixing or multi-line reassembly.
           Companion rule: bounded line length (a packet is a few hundred
           bytes, not one giant blob); the existing PID:LOGDATA? (one
           multi-KB line) is the one current violator and is superseded by
           the stream tier.

        2. Waveform log now logs THROUGH the General-Fault ramp-down.
           pid.c: extracted the log decimation + per-sample write into
           LogDecimationTick()/LogSample() (shared by the normal loop and
           the fault path), and ProcessFaultRampDown() now logs each ramp
           tick. Previously PID_Update() returned early once in FAULT, so
           the log froze at the fault boundary -- a real gap found in the
           demo: GENERAL:TEST:FAULT mid-shot ramped the output 20 kHz ->
           5 kHz, but PID:LOGDATA? showed only the pre-fault flat-top.
           Now setpoint/output follow the ramp (setpointHz is overwritten
           to the ramp value) and measured holds its last value (no
           feedback is consumed during the open-loop ramp).

        Verified on real hardware (two-board controller<->simulator rig,
        freshly flashed): 1750-sample log shows the output ramp 20000 ->
       ~6000 Hz (approaching the 5 kHz floor) after GENERAL:TEST:FAULT,
       with setpoint tracking output and measured held at ~19.76 kHz; all
        four !EVT events (STATE ARMED / STATE FIRING / FAULT GENERAL /
        FAULT CLEAR) surfaced between command replies. Clean build, both
        targets.

2026-09-22  —  wham_console.py handles the !EVT telemetry stream (Phase 5
               partial) + `events` console command
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Host-side counterpart to the Phase 1 telemetry firmware (entry
        above). python/wham_console.py now:

        - Surfaces unsolicited !EVT lines instead of dropping/corrupting
          them: WhamLink.query() drains any pending events before sending,
          then reads in a loop -- printing+logging each !EVT line
          (timestamped with the host's wall-clock) and skipping it -- and
          returns the first non-!EVT line as the actual reply. This is what
          keeps a fault/state event emitted mid-command from being misread
          as that command's OK/ERR reply. Added WhamLink.drain_events()
          (non-blocking) + _emit_event().
        - New `events [0|1|on|off]` console command: no arg shows the
          SYS:EVENT gate (ON/OFF) and drains pending events; 1/0 sends
          SYS:EVENT 1/0. (SYS:EVENT is also still reachable as raw SCPI.)
        - _on_connect() now also queries SYS:TELEM? and prints the schema
          version (tolerates ERR 1 from pre-telemetry firmware).

        Verified: python3 ast.parse clean. NOT yet run against a real board
        (needs the bench: trigger OCP:TEST:FAULT and watch the !EVT line
        print between replies).

2026-09-22  —  Telemetry event stream (Phase 1): SYS:TIME?/SYS:TELEM?/
               SYS:EVENT + unsolicited !EVT state/fault events
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Phase 1 of docs/telemetry.md. New module Core/Inc/telemetry.h +
        Core/Src/telemetry.c: a 64-entry ring buffer carrying timestamped
        state/fault events from the state machine (including ISR context)
        to the main loop, which emits them as unsolicited
        "!EVT <tick> <STATE|FAULT> ..." lines. Push is a few stores inside
        a NESTABLE PRIMASK save/restore critical section (safe from thread
        and ISR context, and nests under the fault path's own existing
        __disable_irq()); emit (Telemetry_PollEmit, main loop) is bounded
        to a few events per iteration and never masks IRQs, so it cannot
        disturb the 1 kHz PID loop or any fault ISR (docs/telemetry.md
        Section 6).

        New commands (commands.c/cmd_parser.c/commands.h):
          SYS:TIME?   -> OK <ms-since-boot>   (HAL_GetTick, 1 ms)
          SYS:TELEM?  -> OK <schema_version>  (TELEMETRY_SCHEMA_VERSION = 1)
          SYS:EVENT <0|1> / SYS:EVENT?         (gate the !EVT stream; default ON)

        state_machine.c now pushes events at every transition: STATE
        (ARMED/FIRING/IDLE on arm/fire/start/stop/disarm/shot-complete),
        FAULT <type> [ch] on fault latch (EnterFault), FAULT CLEAR on
        SM_ClearFault success. Event names mirror STATE?'s own names
        (GENERAL/OVERCURRENT/EXTERNAL_ENABLE/ENABLE_OUTPUT/ENERPRO).

        Memory: +2,064 B .text, +16 B .data, +520 B .bss (64 x 8 B event
        ring). Verified: clean build (zero warnings), both
        BUILD_TARGET_CONTROLLER and BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR. NOT yet
        exercised on real hardware -- needs the bench (trigger a fault,
        observe the !EVT line mid-shot).

        Next phases (docs/telemetry.md): non-blocking TX (Phase 0),
        frozen reply contract (Phase 2), stream standardization (Phase 3),
        flight recorder (Phase 4), wham_console.py telemetry client
        (Phase 5).

2026-09-22  —  FIRE + PID:START gated behind ARM; emergency-stop feature
               removed (design-review follow-up)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Follow-up to a full design review of this project (the same
        session). The review found two of this controller's three
        energization paths -- the legacy `FIRE` command and `PID:START`
        -- could start real output with only a fault check, bypassing
        the ARM/interlock gate that `PID:PROFile:STARt` enforces. Both
        are now gated:

        - PID:START (cmd_pid_start, commands.c): now requires
          SM_STATE_ARMED and SM_ExternalEnableOk() (ERR 13/15), then
          transitions via a new SM_StartPlain() (state_machine.c/.h) --
          ARMED -> FIRING, calling PID_Start(). This loop has no shot
          clock, so PID:STOP (SM_Stop()) is what returns it to IDLE.
        - FIRE (cmd_fire, commands.c): now requires ARMED +
          external-enable (ERR 13/15) in addition to the existing fault
          (ERR 6) and empty-table (ERR 5) checks. Deliberately NOT
          transitioned to FIRING -- the legacy table path is not part of
          the SM's IDLE/ARMED/FIRING lifecycle, and marking it FIRING
          would make EnterFault()'s handlers assume pid.c's PID_Update()
          loop is running when it isn't. ARMED is a required precondition
          only; a fault during playback still safes the legacy output
          (EnterFault from ARMED -> PFM_ForceStop()).

        Also REMOVED the dormant emergency-stop feature entirely, per
        direct decision: SM_FAULT_EMERGENCY_STOP, EMERGency:ENAble/
        ENAble?/INPut?, SM_SetEmergencyStopRequired()/
        SM_GetEmergencyStopRequired()/SM_EmergencyStopOk()/
        SM_GetEmergencyStopInputRaw(), EmergencyStopInputIsHigh()/
        EmergencyStopAsserted()/HandleEmergencyStopFault(), and
        EMERGENCY_STOP_POLARITY (ctrlr_config.h). PG10 -- the feature's
        input pin -- had already been proven electrically tied to NRST
        (a real reset pin, not a usable GPIO; see the 2026-09-17
        entries), so the feature had no pin and no safe way to be wired.
        A future E-stop must use a genuinely free input pin and re-settle
        the fail-safe polarity question afresh.

        Review items TABLED (not implemented) this pass: a loss-of-
        feedback trip/timeout (currently closed-loop holds last output
        with no timeout), and a hard dI/dt (amps/sec) ramp-rate clamp.
        Also flagged during review: PC10/HRTIM1_FLT6 -- the only
        CPU-independent, silicon-level output-safing -- is NOT physically
        connected on this board, so hrtim.c's "even if the CPU is hung"
        comments are currently not true; a watchdog (IWDG) was attempted
        and removed -- it broke serial BOOT/flash (see the next entry).

        Verified: clean build (zero warnings), both BUILD_TARGET_CONTROLLER
        and BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR.

2026-09-22  —  Independent Watchdog (IWDG) added, then REMOVED -- it broke
               serial BOOT/flash (design-review follow-up, part 2)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Added an IWDG (Core/Src/iwdg.c + Core/Inc/iwdg.h, IWDG_ENABLED/
        IWDG_TIMEOUT_MS in ctrlr_config.h, fed from main.c's main loop,
        heartbeat-gated on PID_Update()), then REMOVED it the same day after
        it broke the serial reflash workflow on real hardware.

        Root cause of the breakage (confirmed by direct hardware testing):
        the IWDG, once started, survives NVIC_SystemReset() -- it lives in
        the VDD domain on its own LSI clock, and only a power-cycle stops
        it. The BOOT command's BootJump_RequestBootloader() (boot_jump.c)
        enters the ROM bootloader via exactly that NVIC_SystemReset(), and
        the ROM bootloader does NOT feed a software-started IWDG. So ~1 s
        (IWDG_TIMEOUT_MS) into the bootloader session the IWDG hardware-
        resets the chip mid-flash, corrupting/aborting the reflash and
        leaving the board with no valid app -- and no serial response.
        No software fix exists: the IWDG cannot be stopped by software and
        the ROM bootloader cannot be modified. Removing it is therefore the
        only way to keep the serial BOOT/stm32flash workflow working.

        Removal was complete, not just a flag flip: iwdg.c/iwdg.h deleted;
        main.c's Iwdg_Init()/Iwdg_Refresh() call sites and pid.c's
        Iwdg_NotifyHeartbeat() reverted; IWDG_ENABLED/IWDG_TIMEOUT_MS
        removed from ctrlr_config.h; iwdg.c dropped from
        Debug/Core/Src/subdir.mk + Debug/objects.list.

        Recorded consequence: there is again NO CPU-independent output-
        safing (PC10/HRTIM1_FLT6 is also not physically connected), so a
        CPU hang leaves the demand output at its last command. A watchdog
        can only be reintroduced if the reflash path changes to one that
        resets the IWDG (a power cycle, or a non-BOOT boot mechanism), or
        by abandoning the serial BOOT workflow for SWD/st-flash.

        Verified: clean build (zero warnings), both BUILD_TARGET_CONTROLLER
        and BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR, after the removal.

2026-09-22  —  docs/sop: new Section 9, programming/firing a shot
               profile by hand over raw SCPI -- Rev C
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: a standard operating procedure for a human
        operator programming the controller via raw SCPI, bypassing
        wham_console.py's `shot` wizard entirely. docs/sop/wham_xrex_
        pfmg474_sop.tex was Rev B (2026-09-04), predating this whole
        project's state machine/ARM/PID:PROFile:* system, and only
        covered the legacy TABLE:*/FIRE path -- added a new Section 9
        ("Procedure: Program and Fire a Shot Profile (Raw SCPI)")
        matching its existing style (numbered procedure, CAUTION box,
        acceptance criterion), covering: per-channel setup (XREX:
        CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut, PID:CHANnel:ENAble, PID:LOOPMODE,
        PID:GAINS, PID:PROFile:CURRent), explicitly disabling unused
        channels, PID:PROFile:TIMing (now 3 args, see the ramp-down
        entry above), PID:LOG, ARM/PID:PROFile:STARt, watching for
        completion, PID:LOGDATA?, and resetting to a safe state
        afterward. Added matching Troubleshooting rows (ERR 13 on ARM,
        ERR 12 on PROFile:STARt/TIMing, a "disabled" channel still
        showing data) and a References entry.

        Also corrected two Rev B claims found stale while writing this:
        (1) the Scope section's "no ARM precondition, no state machine,
        no fault interlock" was true only for the legacy path -- the
        modern path gained all three since Rev B; (2) Section 8's own
        caution claimed FIRE has no fault interlock at all, which was
        ALREADY incomplete at Rev B -- cmd_fire() (commands.c) has
        always checked AnyFaultLatched() (ERR 6), it just isn't the
        richer per-category interlock the modern path has. Both
        corrected in place rather than silently left wrong next to new,
        accurate text.

2026-09-22  —  Ramp-up and ramp-down time now independently
               configurable -- was one shared duration for both
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request. Firmware (pid.c/pid.h): split the single
        g_profileRampTicks ("one ramp's length (up == down)") into
        g_profileRampUpTicks/g_profileRampDownTicks; TrapezoidalCurrentA()
        now uses the up-ramp count for its first branch and the
        down-ramp count for its last; g_profileTotalTicks =
        rampUpTicks + flatTopTicks + rampDownTicks (was 2*rampTicks +
        flatTopTicks). PID_SetProfileTiming()/PID_GetProfileTiming()
        now take/return three values (rampUpTimeMs, flatTopTimeMs,
        rampDownTimeMs) instead of two.

        Wire protocol (commands.c): PID:PROFile:TIMing now takes THREE
        arguments (rampUpTimeS flatTopTimeS rampDownTimeS) -- a
        BREAKING change to the wire format; the old two-argument form
        is rejected outright (ERR 12, "needs three arguments"), no
        silent old-format fallback. PID:PROFile:TIMing? now replies
        with three values to match.

        wham_console.py: `shot` wizard gained a third prompt (Ramp Up
        Time / Flat Top Time / Ramp Down Time, was two); `timing`
        wrapper takes three arguments now; self.profile_timing is
        {"ramp_up_s", "flat_s", "ramp_down_s"} (was {"ramp_s", "flat_s"});
        every total_s calculation (do_shot, do_refire's decim fallback,
        _watch_shot) updated from 2*ramp_s+flat_s to
        ramp_up_s+flat_s+ramp_down_s; generate_plot()/generate_multi_
        channel_plot()'s phase-boundary shading and titles updated to
        use ramp_up_s/ramp_down_s separately; _shot_meta()'s
        ramp_time_s meta.json key renamed to ramp_up_time_s, with a new
        ramp_down_time_s alongside it.

        run_simulator_validation.py: start_profile_shot() gained an
        optional ramp_down_s=None parameter (defaults to ramp_s,
        preserving every existing caller's symmetric behavior
        unchanged) and now always sends the new 3-argument PID:PROFile:
        TIMing underneath. The three OTHER call sites in this file that
        send PID:PROFile:TIMing directly (not through start_profile_
        shot()) were updated to the 3-argument form too (ramp_s used
        for both up and down, unchanged behavior) -- required, not
        optional: the old 2-argument form is now rejected by the
        firmware outright, so every caller needed updating regardless
        of whether it wanted asymmetric ramps.

        wham_llm_console.py: updated its own SCPI documentation strings
        (shown to the LLM) to the 3-argument form -- would otherwise
        have kept instructing the LLM to send the now-rejected
        2-argument PID:PROFILE:TIMING.

        docs/command_reference.md updated to match. Rebuilt both
        targets (pid.c/commands.c are shared, compiled into both) and
        reflashed the simulator; the controller flash is still pending
        explicit confirmation per this project's own standing
        convention (its .bin is already built and waiting).

2026-09-22  —  wham_console.py: `refire` now re-arms the waveform log
               before firing -- it was silently re-saving shot 1's
               frozen data on every subsequent refire
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  User-reported: adjusted Ki across three shots (`shot` then
        two `gains ...` + `refire` cycles), watched genuinely different
        live behavior scroll by during each one, but "notice no actual
        change to the output." Checked the three saved shots directly
        -- their allch.csv files were BYTE-FOR-BYTE IDENTICAL (same
        MD5), across all three separately-fired real shots.

        Root cause: `refire` (added earlier the same day) sends ARM +
        PID:PROFile:STARt but never re-sends PID:LOG. PID_GetLogSample
        Count()'s own documented behavior is to keep reporting the
        final count once the log fills (no auto-wrap) -- so once the
        FIRST shot (via `shot`, which does arm logging) filled the
        1000-sample buffer, g_logCount stayed pinned at 1000 forever;
        logThisTick's own `g_logCount < g_logCap` check then stayed
        false on every later tick, so NOTHING was ever written to the
        log again. Every `refire` after that genuinely re-fired the
        real profile (confirmed by the differing live PID:STATus?
        values printed while watching) but then fetched and re-saved
        the exact same frozen shot-1 log, over and over.

        Fixed: `refire` now re-arms PID:LOG (same target/maxSamples/
        decim as whatever `log`/`shot` last armed -- tracked via two
        new instance attributes, last_log_max_samples/last_log_decim)
        immediately before firing. Falls back to recomputing a decim
        from the current profile timing (same auto-sizing formula
        `shot` itself uses) if nothing was ever armed via `log`/`shot`
        this session. Deliberately NOT applied to a raw PID:PROFile:
        STARt typed directly via default() -- that path is intentional
        pure passthrough; an operator scripting raw SCPI needs to
        re-arm PID:LOG themselves between fires, same as on the wire.

        Verified with a real console-driven regression test (pexpect,
        answers only sent after seeing their actual prompt) replaying
        the user's own exact workflow: `shot` (Kp=0.2 Ki=300) then two
        `gains`+`refire` cycles (Ki=400, Ki=1000). All three saved
        CSVs now have different MD5s. Ki=300 vs. 400 -- output jitter
        RMS 417Hz vs. 365Hz (small, expected, matches this project's
        own earlier finding that Ki has to move a lot before jitter
        moves much); Ki=1000 -- 1798Hz, a genuine ~5x jump, exactly
        the Ki-driven-noise relationship established earlier the same
        day. The underlying PID response was never actually stuck --
        only the SAVED DATA was.

2026-09-22  —  PID_ArmLog()/PID_ArmLogAll() now clear the log arrays --
               a disabled channel no longer shows stale data from a
               previous run
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  User-reported (from reviewing a console shot's auto-
        generated plots): channels 2-4, explicitly disabled for that
        shot, showed a full, plausible-looking ramp/flat-top/ramp-down
        trace in both `output` and `measured` instead of sitting flat
        -- read as "the controller is outputting a demand signal on a
        channel that was never enabled," a real safety concern for a
        system that will eventually drive a real Transrex.

        Investigated on real hardware before touching any code (this
        matters too much to guess): fired a shot with channel 2
        explicitly disabled but given a brand-new, never-before-used
        demand current, then watched the SIMULATOR's own raw received
        signal (SIM:CHANnel:STATus? DRIVE_HZ) in real time for the
        full shot -- it stayed at exactly 0 throughout. Real PWM output
        was never the problem: PID_SetChannelEnable() does call
        HRTIM1_SetChannelOutputEnable(), and PID_Update()'s per-channel
        loop `continue`s immediately for any disabled channel
        (st->outputEnabled == 0U), before any setpoint/PID/HRTIM/log
        work happens for it.

        That same `continue` is what actually caused the misleading
        plot, though: PID_ArmLog()/PID_ArmLogAll() only ever reset
        g_logCount/g_logCap/g_logDecim*, never the underlying
        g_logSetpoint/g_logMeasured/g_logOutput arrays themselves --
        so a disabled channel's log slots kept showing whatever was
        written there the LAST time that channel was genuinely active
        (in this case, real data from an entirely different, hours-
        earlier shot). Confirmed directly: PID:PROFile:CURRent 2 2000
        stored and read back correctly, but the logged setpoint for
        that "shot" still ramped to the OLD 5400A target -- because a
        disabled channel never re-enters the update loop to recompute
        anything, so nothing about a fresh PID:PROFile:CURRent could
        ever reach it while disabled.

        Fixed with a new ClearLogArrays() helper (Core/Src/pid.c),
        called from both PID_ArmLog() and PID_ArmLogAll() -- zeroes
        every channel's full PID_LOG_MAX_SAMPLES capacity, not just up
        to the newly-requested `maxSamples`, so no stale data can leak
        through regardless of a future mismatched count. A disabled
        channel's log now reads back as a flat, unambiguous 0 for that
        run, matching the electrical truth instead of contradicting it.

        Verified end-to-end on real hardware after rebuilding+
        reflashing BOTH targets (pid.c is shared, compiled into both):
        fired a real shot with channel 2 genuinely active (real
        4500A-equivalent data logged, 1000/1000 nonzero samples,
        ~76250Hz mid-shot) immediately followed by a second shot with
        channel 2 disabled and channel 1 active instead -- channel 2's
        log came back 0/1000 nonzero, every sample exactly (0,0,0),
        instead of replaying the first shot's real data. Confirms the
        fix, not just "happened to read zero since boot."

2026-09-22  —  Simulator idle tone now ON by default -- passive
               out-of-the-box behavior, no serial setup needed
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: "I want the simulator to be pretty passive
        -- by default it's outputting 3kHz on all channels, and the
        channels respond to pfm inputs assuming all of its conditions
        are met... this would not require serial commands to enable."
        Every piece of this already existed EXCEPT the default:
        SimTransrex_Update()'s gating (ENA_OUT+CONTACT_OUT, read
        straight from hardware, GateDriver_Read()) was already fully
        passive/serial-free; the 100ms-ish low-pass response was
        already SIM_TRANSREX_DEFAULT_TAU_MS=100, configurable live via
        SIM:MODEL:TAU; the 3kHz idle tone itself already existed
        (SIM_TRANSREX_IDLE_TONE_HZ) but defaulted OFF, requiring
        `SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE 1` before an ungated channel showed
        anything but a physically disconnected (silent) output.

        Fixed by flipping s_idleToneEnabled's default (sim_transrex.c)
        from 0 to 1 -- the simulator now boots with all 4 channels
        holding 3kHz, transitioning to a real filtered response the
        instant the controller asserts that channel's ENA_OUT+
        CONTACT_OUT, with zero commands sent to the simulator itself.
        `SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE 0` still exists to go back to the
        original stricter realism (fully silent while ungated) for
        whoever wants it. Updated sim_transrex.h's doc comments
        (SimTransrex_Init()/SimTransrex_Update()/the idle-tone section)
        to match the new default -- they previously described the
        OFF-by-default behavior as the only/normal case.

        Verified end-to-end on real hardware after rebuilding+reflashing
        the simulator (wham_build.py --target simulator, wham_serial_
        flash.py --target simulator): (1) fresh boot, zero serial
        commands sent to the simulator -- SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE? ->
        OK 1, every ungated channel's SIM:CHANnel:STATus? already shows
        FEEDBACK_HZ=3000; (2) drove the CONTROLLER's channel 3 only
        (XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut, PID:CHANnel:ENAble/LOOPMODE/
        GAINS/SETPOINT/START) -- again zero commands to the simulator --
        and polled its STATus?: FEEDBACK_HZ climbed from the 3kHz idle
        tone and converged on the ~40kHz commanded setpoint through the
        filter, confirming the whole passive response chain works with
        the controller as the only thing anyone needs to talk to.

        This also explains today's earlier "simulator not responding"
        report: the wham_console.py `shot` wizard bug fixed earlier the
        same day (never asserting XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut) meant
        the controller was never actually gating any channel on in the
        first place -- the simulator was correctly staying unresponsive
        exactly as designed, given nothing had told it to respond.
