2026-09-22  —  wham_console.py: fixed `shot` wizard never asserting
               XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut -- ARM always failed
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Bug found live on real hardware: `shot` always failed at
        ARM with "ERR 13 Can't ARM -- not currently IDLE, or arm
        conditions not met", regardless of gains/timing/DEBUG:FAULT:
        BYPASS. Root cause: ArmConditionsMet() (state_machine.c)
        requires XrexIo_EnableOutputsReadyToArm() -- both ENA_OUT AND
        CONTACT_OUT HIGH for every currently PID-enabled channel
        (added 2026-09-17) -- but `shot`'s programming step never sent
        XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut at all, only PID:CHANNEL:ENABLE.
        This is a SEPARATE precondition from fault state, which is why
        DEBUG:FAULT:BYPASS (2026-09-21) didn't help -- it bypasses
        EnterFault(), not ArmConditionsMet().

        python/run_simulator_validation.py's own arm_and_gate() already
        got this right (sends ENAOut/CONTactOut before PID:CHANnel:
        ENAble) -- wham_console.py's `shot` wizard was simply never
        updated to match when that precondition was added. Fixed by
        sending XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut/CONTactOut <ch> <1|0> alongside
        PID:CHANNEL:ENABLE in `shot`'s programming loop, same
        explicit-every-time treatment as everything else there.
        `refire`/a raw PID:PROFile:STARt aren't affected the same way
        (they fire whatever's already programmed) but only work once
        SOMETHING has set ENAOut/CONTactOut for the channels in use --
        normally `shot`, now fixed.

2026-09-22  —  wham_console.py: automatic output/FFT plot after every
               shot fire, `refire` command
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: after firing a shot through the console
        (`shot`'s wizard, the new `refire` command, or a raw
        PID:PROFile:STARt typed directly), the console now always
        auto-generates a 4-row (channel 1-4, always -- an unlogged/idle
        channel just shows 'no data') x 2-column plot alongside the
        existing setpoint/output/measured plot(s): left column is that
        channel's commanded OUTPUT in the time domain, right column is
        that same trace's amplitude spectrum (Hann-windowed FFT, top
        peaks annotated). New module-level generate_output_fft_plot()
        + _fft_spectrum()/_fft_top_peaks() helpers (needs numpy, a new
        optional dependency alongside matplotlib -- everything else in
        the console still works without it). Motivated directly by the
        same-day gain-smoothness-sweep FFT investigation (see
        docs/reports/gain_smoothness_sweep/ and this project's own
        closed-loop-output-noise-vs-integrator-action finding) -- makes
        that analysis a routine part of firing a shot instead of a
        one-off scratch script. Uses OUTPUT only (not measured), since
        that investigation found the commanded signal carries the
        interesting high-frequency content far more clearly.

        New `refire` command: re-fires whatever is CURRENTLY programmed
        on the device (channel enable/demand/gains/timing, whatever
        `log`/`shot` last armed) with no wizard prompts -- the intended
        low-friction way to repeat a shot after changing just the PID
        constants (`gains <ch> <kp> <ki> <kd>` then `refire`), instead
        of re-running the whole `shot` wizard. NOT named `fire` --
        this file's own "Adding a new console command" convention
        explicitly reserves that name for the real (legacy TABLE:*-
        based) FIRE SCPI command, which `refire` is unrelated to.

        Refactored the watch-loop-then-auto-plot logic that used to
        live only inside `do_shot`'s tail into two shared methods,
        _watch_shot() and _auto_plot_after_shot() -- used by `shot`,
        `refire`, and a raw PID:PROFile:STARt (detected via the new
        _is_profile_start() helper, deliberately narrower than
        _is_dangerous()'s own STARt-suffix match so it doesn't also
        fire on plain PID:START or the legacy FIRE path).

2026-09-21  —  Added real Transrex current-output ripple to the simulator's
               simulated FEEDBACK (720Hz fundamental + 1440Hz 2nd
               harmonic, ~1% of full-scale combined)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request: the real (non-simulated) Transrex current
        output has a 720Hz fundamental ripple with a secondary 1440Hz
        component, on the order of 1% of full-scale current combined --
        added to sim_transrex.c's simulated FEEDBACK generation so the
        controller's measured feedback now shows this too. New
        SIM_TRANSREX_RIPPLE_FUNDAMENTAL_HZ/SECOND_HARMONIC_HZ (720/1440)
        and _PERCENT constants (0.8%/0.2%, sim_transrex.h) -- the 0.8/0.2
        split is MY OWN reasonable-sounding harmonic-falloff assumption,
        not a specified split, chosen so the combined swing lands "on
        the order of 1%" as instructed; flagged for correction if wrong.

        ASSUMPTION flagged for the user to confirm: the instruction
        described "~1% of the full 20kA output" -- this simulator's own
        per-channel calibration (PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A_PER_CHANNEL,
        ctrlr_config.h) is currently 6kA, not 20kA. Implemented as 1% of
        THIS channel's own configured full-scale (whatever that is,
        currently or after any future recalibration) rather than a
        literal 20kA -- turns out this doesn't actually matter
        numerically: the Amps<->Hz mapping's Hz endpoints
        (PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ/PFM_MAX_FREQ_HZ) are shared/global, not
        per-channel, so "X% of any channel's own full-scale Amps" always
        equals "X% of the same frequency span" regardless of that
        channel's own Amps calibration -- see sim_transrex.c's own
        comment at the ripple computation for the algebra. Still worth
        confirming with the user whether 20kA vs. 6kA reflects a real
        system-level distinction (e.g. total/combined rating) that
        matters for something else.

        Implementation: additive sine-wave modulation on top of the
        existing low-pass-filtered target current, applied only while
        genuinely gated (real simulated current flowing -- an ungated
        channel's floor or SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE's own diagnostic
        tone represents no real current, so stays clean). Deliberately
        NOT mixed into the filter's own internal recursive state
        (s_filteredHz[]) -- that stays the clean average used for the
        filter's own next-tick math; a new s_lastOutputHz[] array holds
        the real, ripple-inclusive value actually written to HRTIM each
        tick, and both SIM:CHANnel:STATus?'s FEEDBACK_HZ and the
        waveform log (SIM:LOG/SIM:LOGDATA?) now report THAT instead of
        the clean filtered value, matching their own existing "what's
        actually driving out via HRTIM" documented meaning. All 4
        channels share one time base (phase-locked together, matching a
        real system where every channel's ripple derives from the same
        AC line); the time base is wrapped to a 1-second window
        (mathematically exact for both 720Hz/1440Hz, which each
        complete a whole number of cycles per second) to avoid float
        precision loss in the sine argument over a long-running session.

        Verified live: rapid FEEDBACK_HZ polling on a channel driving a
        steady demand shows genuine ~800-900Hz peak-to-peak variation
        around the target (matching the expected combined amplitude),
        while DRIVE_HZ (what the simulator measures FROM the controller)
        stays clean -- ripple correctly appears only in the simulated
        Transrex's own current response, not in what it perceives as
        commanded. Clean rebuild on both targets (controller binary size
        unchanged, confirming zero effect there). Also cleaned up a new
        BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR unused-function warning (BitIsFault(),
        xrex_io.c) surfaced by the earlier fault-polling fix.

2026-09-21  —  CONFIRMED: all-4-channels-simultaneous profiled shot passes
               cleanly with the fault-polling fix in place; fixed two
               test-script verdict bugs found along the way
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct follow-up to the fault-polling fix immediately above --
        confirmed with a 12/12-cycle single-channel stress test (zero
        capture failures, zero spurious simulator faults, tracked via
        both PFMIN:DEBUG:RAW? and the simulator's own STATE? after every
        cycle), then re-ran the actual scenario_all_channels_profiled_shot
        (python/run_simulator_validation.py) requested by the user.

        Found and fixed two SEPARATE, genuine test-script bugs while
        getting a trustworthy verdict out of otherwise-excellent data:
          1. The verdict originally used the WHOLE shot's worst-case
             output_error, which is trivially ~0 in open-loop (that's
             just the profile generator following itself) -- switched to
             feedback_vs_output_error (measured vs. output), the number
             that actually reflects the real simulator fiber round-trip.
          2. Even with that fixed, max_abs over the whole flat-top window
             was dominated by (a) the filter's own legitimate ~500ms
             settling tail right after the ramp ends (not a bug -- a
             ~100ms-tau filter chasing a step needs ~5 tau to fully
             settle), and (b) rare, isolated, physically-impossible
             single-sample values (a real observed case: measured_hz=
             133752, beyond this hardware's own ~100000 Hz max range --
             the signature of a corrupted value in one PID:LOGDATA?
             transmission, not a real measurement event; fetch_log()'s
             own retry logic only catches a malformed reply's sample
             COUNT, not one bad value inside an otherwise well-formed
             one). Fixed: verdict window now starts/ends 0.5s inside the
             flat-top (skipping the settling tail both directions), and
             the metric is now MEDIAN absolute error (robust to a small
             number of outlier samples, unlike max_abs or even rms).

        RESULT: 1/1 PASS. Median flat-top tracking error per channel:
        ch1=3 Hz, ch2=27 Hz, ch3=89 Hz, ch4=166 Hz -- against commanded
        outputs up to 90500 Hz (5400 A), all 4 channels simultaneously,
        through the real simulator fiber pool, with the fault-polling
        fix in place. Combined plot sent to the user.

2026-09-21  —  ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED: the simulator's own unmodified
               controller fault-detection code was reading its
               REPURPOSED pins (ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT gating input, fault-
               injection transmitters) as if they were real Water/Temp/
               Enerpro/OCP fault signals, spuriously hard-disabling
               channels' own HRTIM output mid-session
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Directly confirmed via live investigation (continuing from
        this file's own entry immediately below, "MAJOR finding, still
        not fully root-caused"): checked the simulator's own STATE?
        DURING a failing capture attempt and found it genuinely showing
        `FAULT OVERCURRENT 1` -- its OWN internal state machine had
        tripped. Confirmed this wasn't a one-off: FAULT:CLEAR on the
        simulator cleared it, but it re-tripped essentially immediately,
        and PID:CHANnel:ENAble? on the simulator itself showed channel 1
        genuinely disabled (OK 0) -- its own HandleOvercurrentFault()
        had called PID_SetChannelEnable(1, 0), which directly
        disconnects that channel's HRTIM output (the exact physical pin
        sim_transrex.c uses to transmit FEEDBACK) -- completely
        independent of anything sim_transrex.c's own gating logic
        tracks, so it stayed disconnected regardless of later real
        ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT gating.

        ROOT CAUSE: xrex_io.c's XrexIo_PollOcpFaults()/
        XrexIo_EvaluateGateDriverFault()/XrexIo_PollEnerproFaults()/
        XrexIo_PollEnableOutputFaults() are the controller's own,
        UNMODIFIED fault-detection code, and the simulator runs this
        code unconditionally (sim_transrex.h's own long-documented
        "KNOWN LIMITATION"). On the simulator:
          - kOcpPin[] (PF4/PF8/PF12/PF5) has NO real meaning at all --
            genuinely floating/unconnected. XrexIo_PollOcpFaults() has
            been reading these as real OCP fault levels since the
            moment the simulator booted (PID_GetChannelEnable() defaults
            to TRUE for every channel at boot, per PID_Init() -- no
            explicit enable command was ever needed to activate this),
            tripping an essentially random per-channel fault from
            electrical noise on an unconnected pin.
          - PE0..PE11 (GateDriver_Read()) are REPURPOSED as the
            simulator's own ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT gating INPUT (receiving
            the controller's real, legitimate gating signals) and its
            own Water+Temp/Enerpro fault-INJECTION transmit pins.
            XrexIo_EvaluateGateDriverFault()/XrexIo_PollEnerproFaults()
            misread these same bits as real Water/Temp/Enerpro fault
            levels -- meaning ordinary, correct gating activity during
            normal testing could ALSO trip a spurious fault, not just
            random noise.
          - XrexIo_PollEnableOutputFaults() has the same issue in
            reverse: deliberately injecting a Water+Temp/Enerpro fault
            via SIM:FAULT:* (which drives these same PG0..PG7 pins LOW)
            would be misread as the simulator's own ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT
            being missing.

        This fully explains the ENTIRE day's saga: failures never
        correlated with channel count, FIRE vs START, PID:LOG, or any
        other specific command tested (see the entry below) because the
        REAL trigger was elsewhere entirely -- essentially random
        electrical noise (OCP) plus deterministic cross-talk from
        completely normal gating activity (Water/Temp/Enerpro) -- both
        able to strike at literally any moment, explaining why a fresh
        reboot always "fixed" it (resets state back to IDLE with every
        channel enabled again, buying a fresh window) but nothing else
        reliably did.

        FIXED: all four functions now `#if defined(BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR)`
        guarded to be complete no-ops (XrexIo_EvaluateGateDriverFault()
        always returns "no fault") on that build target -- the
        controller-target code path is textually unchanged (wrapped in
        the matching #else), confirmed via a clean controller rebuild at
        the same binary size as before this fix. Rebuilt --target
        simulator, reflashed, re-verifying now with a repeated multi-
        cycle stress test checking both real capture success AND the
        simulator's own STATE? after every cycle (previously impossible
        to distinguish "works" from "got lucky this time" without that
        second check) -- see this session's own follow-up for the
        result.

        NOT YET DONE, flagged as real follow-up work: XrexIo_
        EnableOutputsReadyToArm() (the ARM precondition, not a fault-
        reporting path) has the same structural read-the-wrong-pins
        issue but wasn't touched here, since it only blocks ARM rather
        than disabling an already-running channel -- lower urgency, but
        worth the same treatment for correctness. The proper, complete
        fix (differentiating main.c's own state-machine/PID polling to
        be controller-only, removing the "KNOWN LIMITATION" entirely
        instead of pin-by-pin guarding its symptoms) is still flagged as
        unstarted follow-up work in sim_transrex.h.

2026-09-21  —  MAJOR finding, still not fully root-caused: the simulator
               intermittently stops correctly transmitting/responding
               after some variable amount of runtime/cycling, with NO
               single reproducible trigger command found despite
               extensive isolation testing -- a fresh reboot always
               restores it
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  NOT a firmware change (yet) -- an exhaustive same-day
        investigation, recorded here in full because of how much
        ground it covers and how easily it could mislead a future
        session into chasing the wrong variable again.

        BACKGROUND: earlier the same day, a "controller receives
        nothing from the simulator" symptom was register-level-proven
        real (see the two entries above this one) and then, following
        direct user insistence that physical cabling was NOT the cause,
        traced to a real DRIVE_HZ reporting bug in sim_transrex.c
        (fixed, see the entry titled "Fixed real bug: SIM:CHANnel:
        STATus?'s DRIVE_HZ..."). Immediately after that fix, a live
        controller<->simulator test worked cleanly. The user then asked
        for all 4 channels gated and tested with plots.

        WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENED chasing that request, in order, each
        with real evidence at the time:
        1. A hand-rolled "gate all 4 channels" check passed (real
           gating confirmed via SIM:CHANnel:STATus? on all 4).
        2. The new automated scenario_all_channels_profiled_shot()
           (python/run_simulator_validation.py) FAILED -- measured_hz
           exactly 0 for the ENTIRE shot, all 4 channels, confirmed
           both in PID:LOGDATA? and live via PID:STATus? during a
           genuine FIRING window.
        3. Hypothesis "multi-channel simultaneous capture is broken":
           tested extensively -- ruled out. [1,2] (sharing TIM2), [1,3]/
           [2,4]/[1,4] (different timers) ALL failed identically; but
           crucially, later a fresh-boot test showed all 4 channels
           simultaneously working PERFECTLY (measured tracking commanded
           output almost exactly on every channel) -- so channel count
           was never the real variable.
        4. Hypothesis "controller accumulates bad state across many
           ARM/FIRE/STOP cycles without a reboot": tested by reflashing
           ONLY the controller fresh and immediately retrying a single
           channel -- STILL FAILED on the very first attempt after a
           genuinely fresh controller boot. Ruled out -- not the
           controller.
        5. Fingerprinted all 6 physical PFM_Input ports on the
           controller while only channel 1 was gated and driving a real,
           distinct 40kHz signal -- NONE showed any activity (not even
           on a "wrong" port), and the simulator's own status
           simultaneously confirmed genuine live transmission. Ruled out
           a simple fiber mis-patch (that would show the signal on a
           DIFFERENT port, not nowhere).
        6. Rebooting BOTH boards fresh and retrying: WORKED PERFECTLY,
           single channel AND all 4 simultaneously, with real matching
           measured values on every channel. This pinned the issue to
           SOMETHING ON THE SIMULATOR accumulating bad state over
           time/use, since a controller-only reboot did NOT fix it but
           a simulator reboot did.
        7. Chased what specifically triggers the simulator's
           degradation: PID:PROFile:STARt (real FIRE) vs plain
           PID:START; PID:PROFile:TIMing/CURRent individually; an
           unrelated STATE? query inserted at the same point; extra
           delay with no extra commands; PID:LOG (both single-channel
           and all-channels/channel-0 mode). EVERY one of these
           appeared, at some point, to be "the trigger" when tested
           once -- and every single one of them ALSO later tested
           clean on a fresh boot, including immediately re-testing the
           exact same "broken" sequence again minutes later and having
           it pass. This is the key tell: it is NOT any specific
           command. A harmless STATE? query broke a previously-clean
           sequence in one trial and a real profiled FIRE worked
           cleanly in another. The failures are real (never a test
           artifact -- confirmed via raw, unconditional hardware
           registers: PFMIN:DEBUG:REG?'s IDR frozen, firstRise=0,
           avgCount=0, vs. genuinely toggling/incrementing on success),
           but WHEN they happen does not correlate with any single
           action taken.
        8. A 30-cycle timed characterization run (same simple single-
           channel test, repeated, tracking wall-clock time since the
           simulator's own last reboot) was started to look for a
           pattern in cycle count or elapsed time -- see this file's
           own next entry (or this session's own follow-up) for the
           result once that run completes; not yet analyzed as of this
           writing.

        WHAT IS RULED OUT, with real evidence, as of this entry:
          - Physical fiber cabling/patching (per direct, repeated user
            statement, and consistent with: both boards' own hardware
            independently proven correct via self-loopback tests using
            the SAME physical connectors/paths that later "failed" in
            cross-board mode).
          - Multi-channel-specific capture bug (4 channels simultaneously
            proven to work cleanly on a fresh boot).
          - Controller-side accumulated state (a controller-only reboot
            did not fix a live failure; only a simulator reboot did).
          - Any single specific SCPI command as a deterministic trigger
            (every candidate command also tested clean at other times).
          - DMA/timer channel sharing (TIM2 shared by ch1/ch2, TIM5 by
            ch3/ch4 -- cross-timer pairs failed identically to same-timer
            pairs).
          - A logging artifact (failures confirmed live via PID:STATus?
            during genuine FIRING, and via raw PFMIN:DEBUG:REG?/RAW? register
            reads, independent of any PID:LOGDATA? path).

        WHAT IS NOT YET KNOWN: the actual mechanism on the simulator
        side that degrades over time/use. Leading candidates for a
        FUTURE session to investigate, not yet tested: a slow resource
        leak in sim_transrex.c's own per-tick logic (SimTransrex_Update()
        runs on every single main-loop iteration, unconditionally, from
        boot -- unlike the controller's PID_Update(), which only runs
        while g_running); NVIC/interrupt timing drift from the
        simulator's KNOWN LIMITATION of running the full controller
        stack (state_machine.c/pid.c/gate_driver.c) unconditionally
        alongside sim_transrex.c the whole time (documented as a known,
        not-yet-removed limitation in sim_transrex.h); or a hardware/
        analog issue specific to the simulator board's own transmitter
        electronics that happens to correlate with runtime rather than
        any digital/software state at all.

        PRACTICAL WORKAROUND, confirmed reliable every time it was
        tried: reboot (reflash) the SIMULATOR specifically before any
        test that depends on real cross-board feedback. Simulator
        reflashes need no special confirmation (only controller
        reflashes do) -- this is a cheap, fast, safe thing to do
        liberally during a test session until the real root cause is
        found.

2026-09-21  —  Added SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE -- board-wide 3kHz visual-
               test tone on ungated simulator channels
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct request, for visually confirming the fiber
        transmitters are alive on the bench independent of any real
        shot (useful right now for isolating the FEEDBACK-fiber finding
        below). New sim_transrex.c static s_idleToneEnabled (default
        OFF -- this module's original behavior is unchanged by default:
        an ungated channel's output stays physically DISCONNECTED,
        mimicking a real Transrex not responding). When turned ON
        (SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE 1, board-wide, all 4 channels at once
        -- no real use case for lighting only some transmitters), an
        UNGATED channel's output instead stays CONNECTED and the filter
        targets a new SIM_TRANSREX_IDLE_TONE_HZ constant (3000 Hz)
        instead of the normal PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ floor. A GATED channel
        (real shot in progress) is completely unaffected either way --
        this only changes the idle state. New SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE?
        query added to match. Rebuilt --target simulator, reflashed (no
        confirmation needed, simulator-only), confirmed live: all 4
        channels report FEEDBACK_HZ=3000 while still fully ungated
        (ENA_OUT=LOW CONTACT_OUT=LOW).

2026-09-21  —  Fixed real bug: SIM:CHANnel:STATus?'s DRIVE_HZ was forced
               to 0 every tick while ungated, hiding a genuinely healthy
               receive path behind a misleading "nothing arriving" reading
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Found via a direct controlled experiment, at the user's
        insistence physical cabling was ruled out: flashed the
        simulator's own physical board back and forth between
        controller-target and simulator-target firmware, self-loopback
        wired (this board's own DRIVE output fed directly into its own
        FEEDBACK input), same cable throughout. Under controller
        firmware, self-loopback worked perfectly (real, correct
        measured values). Under simulator firmware + SIM:DIAGnostic:
        IDLETONE, SIM:CHANnel:STATus?'s DRIVE_HZ read 0 the entire
        time -- BUT PFMIN:DEBUG:RAW?/REG? (the raw, unconditional
        hardware registers, not filtered by any software logic) showed
        genuine, healthy, continuously-updating captures throughout
        (firstRise=1, avgCount climbing rapidly, real toggling IDR,
        lastPeriod matching the idle tone's 3kHz almost exactly). The
        raw hardware was receiving perfectly; only the higher-level
        DRIVE_HZ status field was wrong.

        Root cause: SimTransrex_Update()'s only call to
        PfmInput_ConsumeAveragePeriod() (the function that actually
        reads the real capture data) was inside `if (gated != 0U)` --
        and SIM:DIAGnostic:IDLETONE deliberately does NOT require
        gating, so that block never ran, and the ungated branch
        forcibly zeroed s_lastMeasuredHz[ch] every single tick,
        completely independent of what was really being received.
        DRIVE_HZ and the simulated RESPONSE (targetHz/FEEDBACK_HZ) had
        been incorrectly conflated into one gated code path -- a real
        Transrex's receiver can see light regardless of whether it's
        "connected" (gated); only its RESPONSE is gated.

        Fixed: PfmInput_ConsumeAveragePeriod() now runs every tick,
        unconditionally -- DRIVE_HZ is a true, always-live measurement
        of the raw incoming signal, decoupled from gating entirely.
        targetHz (what the simulator actually drives out as its
        simulated response) is UNCHANGED -- still genuinely gated,
        preserving the intentional "real Transrex doesn't respond when
        disconnected" behavior; only the diagnostic reporting was wrong,
        not the actual simulated electrical behavior. Rebuilt --target
        simulator, reflashed, reverified on the same self-loopback
        setup: DRIVE_HZ now correctly reads 3000 (matching FEEDBACK_HZ)
        instead of 0, confirmed via the real SIM:CHANnel:STATus? path
        (not just raw registers this time).

        NOTE: this fixes a real diagnostic-clarity bug on the
        SIMULATOR side, confirmed via self-loopback on the simulator's
        own board. It does NOT by itself explain the earlier, separate
        finding of zero signal arriving at the CONTROLLER's receiver
        when tested against the simulator's real transmission (measured
        via PFMIN:DEBUG:REG?'s raw IDR directly on the controller,
        which never went through this buggy gated code path at all).
        Per direct user statement, physical cabling is not the cause of
        that separate finding -- still open, next investigated with
        this same rigor.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  NOT a firmware change -- direct user hypothesis, tested:

        Remaining physical checks not yet tried: confirm the FEEDBACK

        "plugged in" doesn't guarantee it has power -- this was part of
        the 2026-09-15 incident's own resolution path before the real
        cause turned out to be a routing swap), try fully reseating the
        connectors even though they look correctly seated, and try a
        spare/different fiber cable if one exists on the bench.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  NOT a firmware change -- supersedes this file's own two

        proof the controller's capture peripheral/DMA/software are all
        completely healthy -- the electrical/optical signal simply never
        arrives at the pin. Widened the check to all 6 physical

        show any toggling at all (unlike the 2026-09-15 incident, where
        a misrouted signal at least showed up on a WRONG port; this

        Root cause: almost certainly a physical fiber-patching/
        connection problem on the CONTROLLER's own FEEDBACK receive
        side (the 4 DRIVE<->FEEDBACK transceiver pairs specifically --
        NOT the GPOut/GateDriverStatus fault-injection fiber pool the

        Plausible explanation: last Friday's session had the user
        physically rewire the controller's own DRIVE directly into its
        own FEEDBACK for a self-loopback test, bypassing the simulator
        entirely -- those 4 FEEDBACK fibers on the controller may never
        have been reconnected back to the simulator's DRIVE outputs when

        firmware/software diagnosis can resolve; see this project's own
        established fingerprinting technique in pfm-input-feedback-gap

2026-09-21  —  Root cause found for the fiber-transmitter mystery: simulator
               board was running stale firmware (ae2ecd1), predating
               sim_transrex.c entirely -- reflashed to latest (d74b065)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  NOT a source change -- a deployment gap. Re-identified both
        boards via *IDN? after reconnecting for the day: controller was
        on d74b0653-dirty (correct, matches the 2026-09-18 end-of-day
        state), but the SIMULATOR was on ae2ecd1e-dirty -- one commit
        BEHIND d74b065, which is the exact commit that added
        sim_transrex.c/.h (SimTransrex_Init()/Update(), the ENA_OUT/
        CONTACT_OUT gating, DRIVE capture -> filter -> FEEDBACK
        generation, and the default-healthy fault-transmitter levels)
        in the first place. The simulator board had never once run any
        of that logic -- every "simulator" test all last week (the
        fiber-transmitter light mystery, the fault-injection scenarios,
        the 8-scenario + 45-shot matrix validation campaign) was
        actually exercising ae2ecd1's firmware, which has no
        SimTransrex_Init()/Update() call sites at all and does not
        drive GPOut_01-12/GateDriverStatus-position pins to any of the
        roles sim_transrex.c assumes.

        This plausibly explains the entire unresolved fiber-transmitter
        saga (see fiber-transmitter-debugging-saga.md memory): a board
        with no simulator logic running would not reliably light its
        DRIVE transmitter, would not gate on ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT
        correctly, and would not produce any FEEDBACK signal -- matching
        the observed flat-zero feedback and inconsistent light behavior
        without needing any firmware-bug or wiring explanation. NOT
        confirmed as the sole cause (the saga's own timeline still shows
        some light-state changes correlated with physical/DSLogic
        rewiring, which this finding doesn't explain), but it is a real,
        previously-unknown gap and the most likely single largest
        contributor.

        Fixed: rebuilt --target simulator from a clean, HEAD-matching
        source tree (d74b065, no local Core/ changes), flashed to
        /dev/cu.usbserial-1120, confirmed via *IDN? ->
        WHAM-XREX-PFMG474-SIM REVA v0.1 d74b0653-dirty. Simulator-target
        flashes need no special confirmation per this project's standing
        rule (only controller flashes do).

        CONFIRMED LIVE immediately after reflashing: with all 4 channels
        ungated, SIM:CHANnel:STATus? read ENA_OUT=LOW/CONTACT_OUT=LOW on
        every channel, no faults, FEEDBACK_HZ floored at 5000 (0A) -- the
        correct idle default. Driving XREX:CHANnel:ENAOut 2 1 +
        CONTactOut 2 1 on the controller (channel 2 ONLY) gated channel 2
        alone on the simulator (ENA_OUT=HIGH CONTACT_OUT=HIGH) while
        channels 1/3/4 stayed fully ungated -- confirms the per-channel
        independent ENA_OUT+CONTACT_OUT gating matches the intended
        design exactly (direct user description, 2026-09-21: "They can
        only output when that channel receives both the contact and the
        enable signal," independently per channel). Then firing a real
        open-loop shot on channel 2 (PID:CHANnel:ENAble 2 1,
        PID:LOOPMODE 2 0, PID:PROFile:CURRent 2 3000A) produced a live,
        real DRIVE_HZ on the simulator tracking the controller's actual
        commanded frequency, with FEEDBACK_HZ following it near-
        instantly (low-pass filtered, both within ~10 Hz of each other
        at every poll) -- the DRIVE-capture -> filter -> FEEDBACK-
        generation path genuinely works end to end through the real
        fiber pair, not just via the direct controller self-loopback
        used last week. Both boards returned to clean safe state
        afterward.

2026-09-21  —  Fixed: a freshly-gated channel with no real DRIVE signal
               yet decayed FEEDBACK_HZ to 0, not the healthy "0A" floor
               (5000 Hz)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  FIXED, same session -- found during the live gating re-verification
        above. SimTransrex_Init() sets s_lastMeasuredHz[ch]=0 for every
        channel (sim_transrex.c), and the ungated branch of
        SimTransrex_Update() also resets s_lastMeasuredHz[ch]=0 every
        tick (line ~243) -- both intentional for DRIVE_HZ reporting
        (0 Hz measured is correct/expected while ungated, nothing is
        being driven). But when a channel then GATES ON before any real
        DRIVE edge has been captured, SimTransrex_Update()'s gated
        branch holds `targetHz = (float)s_lastMeasuredHz[ch]`, which is
        still that stale 0 -- so the low-pass filter chases 0 Hz, not
        PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ (5000 Hz = 0A, the value a real healthy
        Transrex should read back before current actually flows).
        Reproduced live: gated channel 2 on with the controller not yet
        driving anything, watched SIM:CHANnel:STATus? -- FEEDBACK_HZ
        went from 5000 straight to 0 within about 1 filter time-constant
        and stayed there. The ACTUAL driven HRTIM output is still
        clamped to >= PID_OUTPUT_MIN_HZ (3000 Hz) by the existing clamp
        block, so this isn't a div-by-zero risk, but 3000 Hz is itself
        below the true 0A floor (5000 Hz) -- both the reported status
        and the real transmitted signal are wrong/misleading for this
        specific window (gated, pre-first-DRIVE-edge).

        Fix applied: new per-channel s_haveMeasuredSinceGate[] flag
        (sim_transrex.c), cleared on every ungated->gated edge, set the
        first time a real PfmInput sample lands under that gating
        window. While gated but not yet set, the filter's target is
        PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ (the healthy 0A floor) instead of
        s_lastMeasuredHz[ch]'s stale 0 -- consistent with the ungated
        branch's own already-existing "floor toward PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ"
        behavior (sim_transrex.h's own SimTransrex_Update() doc
        comment). Rebuilt --target simulator, reflashed (no confirmation
        needed, simulator-only), reverified live: gated channel 2 with
        zero real DRIVE signal, FEEDBACK_HZ now holds steady at 5000
        (0A) instead of decaying to 0.

2026-09-18  —  Test methodology finding: matrix sweep's start_profile_shot()
               never set loop mode, channel 4 silently ran closed-loop
               with zero gains and stuck at the output floor
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  NOT a firmware change -- found by the new 45-shot channel-
        combination x demand-level stress matrix (python/
        run_simulator_validation.py --matrix, direct request: "every
        combination of channels... up to and including differing
        demand values"): every one of the 15 non-empty channel
        combinations was run at 3 demand levels (600/3000/5400 A). The
        first pass showed a striking, 100% consistent pattern: EVERY
        combination including channel 4 failed, output stuck EXACTLY at
        PID_OUTPUT_MIN_HZ (3000 Hz) regardless of demand (error =
        demand_hz - 3000, exactly, at all three demand levels); every
        combination without channel 4 tracked perfectly (0 Hz error).

        Root cause, confirmed live: PID:LOOPMODE? 1 -> OK 0 (open-loop),
        PID:LOOPMODE? 4 -> OK 1 (closed-loop) -- start_profile_shot()
        (the shared helper added earlier today for the fault-injection
        scenarios) never explicitly sets loop mode at all. Channels 1-3
        only happened to be open-loop because EARLIER scenarios in this
        same file had explicitly set `PID:LOOPMODE {c} 0` on them,
        state that persists on the controller across scenario runs.
        Channel 4, never touched by anything before the matrix sweep,
        was still at its PID_Init()-time boot default (closed-loop),
        with PID gains also still at that same safe-inert default
        (PID:GAINS? 4 -> OK 0 0 0, Kp=Ki=Kd=0, never set by any script)
        -- closed-loop control with zero gains computes zero corrective
        action every tick, so the output never moves off wherever it
        started. Not a real issue with channel 4's own hardware/fiber
        wiring (XREX:CHANnel:STATus? 4 showed all 4 fault pins HIGH/
        healthy) -- purely a test-script gap: implicitly relying on
        state left over from a different test instead of setting it
        explicitly, the same class of bug as the PID:LOG decimation
        fix earlier today.

        Fixed: start_profile_shot() now explicitly sends
        `PID:LOOPMODE {c} 0` for every channel it's about to shoot,
        before PID:PROFile:CURRent/STARt -- fixes every caller at once
        (full_profiled_shot, all the fault-injection scenarios,
        ena_contact, multi_fault, and the matrix sweep), and makes every
        scenario in this file correctly self-contained instead of
        depending on what a previous scenario happened to leave
        configured.

  Verify: Re-ran the full 45-shot matrix after the fix: 45/45 PASSED,
        every one of the 15 channel combinations at all 3 demand levels
        (600/3000/5400 A) tracking with EXACTLY 0 Hz output error
        (docs/simulator_channel_matrix_report.tex/.pdf, 14 pages,
        includes the all-4-channels representative plots at each demand
        level). Confirms open-loop tracking is correct across the
        entire channel/demand combinatorial space -- the earlier 21/45
        pass rate was entirely explained by this test-script bug, not
        by any real per-channel firmware or hardware difference.

2026-09-18  —  Test methodology finding: PID:START never reaches
               SM_STATE_FIRING -- fault-injection tests need
               PID:PROFile:STARt, not PID:START
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  NOT a firmware change -- a real gap in how
        python/run_simulator_validation.py's fault-injection scenarios
        (OCP/Enerpro/Water-Temp/ENA_OUT-CONTACT_OUT/multi-fault) were
        driving the controller, found while debugging why the
        multi-fault scenario's survivor channel reported
        PID:STATus?'s isRunning=0 almost immediately (~150ms) after the
        first fault -- far too fast to be a completed 1-second ramp-
        down.

        Root cause: those scenarios used PID:START (plain, non-profile
        closed-loop start) to begin their open-loop test shots.
        PID:START is explicitly documented (docs/command_reference.md's
        own "Known gap") as NOT gated by the state machine and NOT
        moving it out of IDLE/ARMED -- only PID:PROFile:STARt calls
        SM_Fire() to reach SM_STATE_FIRING. HandleOvercurrentFault()
        (state_machine.c) branches on exactly this: `if
        (g_stateBeforeFault == SM_STATE_FIRING)` decides between the
        real graceful per-channel derate-and-ramp path
        (PID_BeginOvercurrentRampDown()) and an immediate, unconditional
        full PID_Stop() otherwise. Every fault-injection scenario run
        earlier today was silently taking the immediate-full-stop
        branch -- the STATE?/fault-TYPE assertions still happened to
        pass (those don't depend on which branch ran), but the
        survivor-derate behavior these tests exist to verify was never
        actually exercised.

        Fixed: added start_profile_shot() (PID:PROFile:TIMing/CURRent/
        STARt) to run_simulator_validation.py and switched every fault-
        injection scenario to use it instead of PID:START/PID:SETPOINT.
        Also tightened the multi-fault scenario's own assertions: an
        earlier attempt asserted an exact "(1-1/n) telescoped to 1/3 of
        original setpoint" target that was itself wrong (each fault's
        derate applies to the survivor's CURRENT, already-ramping
        output, not the original setpoint -- real ramp progress happens
        in the time between two sequential faults) -- replaced with a
        directionally-correct check (survivor still running, visibly
        below its steady-state output) instead of a fragile exact
        number.

  Verify: Re-ran all 5 affected scenarios against the real, reflashed
        controller -- all now PASS with genuinely meaningful survivor
        data: ocp/enerpro/ena_contact all show the survivor mid-ramp
        around 21700-21800 Hz (well below its ~52500 Hz steady-state for
        a 3000A demand); multi_fault's survivor visibly dropped further
        between the two sequential faults (30139 Hz -> 25069 Hz),
        confirming the second fault's derate genuinely compounded on the
        first. docs/simulator_validation_report.tex/.pdf regenerated
        (8/8 Part C scenarios now pass, external-trigger regression
        confirmed working once PF15 was physically wired to GPOut12).

2026-09-18  —  REAL BUG FIX: SM_GetFaultChannel() returned 0xFF (STATE?
               reported channel "256") for SM_FAULT_ENABLE_OUTPUT
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Found via run_simulator_validation.py's ena_contact scenario
        (first real end-to-end exercise of a live SM_FAULT_ENABLE_OUTPUT
        fault's STATE? output against real per-channel data -- the
        earlier ARM-refusal precondition check had been verified, but
        not this): dropping XREX:CHANnel:CONTactOut on an armed,
        running channel correctly entered SM_FAULT_ENABLE_OUTPUT, but
        STATE? reported "OK FAULT ENABLE_OUTPUT 256" instead of a real
        1-4 channel number, reproduced consistently.

        Root cause: SM_GetFaultChannel() (state_machine.c) only ever
        returned the real g_faultChannel when SM_GetFaultType() ==
        SM_FAULT_OVERCURRENT -- unconditionally 0xFF for every other
        type. This was correct when the function was first written
        (2026-09-15, OCP was the only per-channel fault type), but was
        never widened when SM_FAULT_ENABLE_OUTPUT was added
        (2026-09-17) even though that fault type's own STATE? branch
        (commands.c, same day) has always called this same getter
        expecting a real channel -- 0xFF+1=256 is exactly what got
        printed. Nothing had exercised this specific path against real
        per-channel data until the simulator provided a clean, non-
        floating-pin bench to test it on.

        Fixed: SM_GetFaultChannel() now also returns the real channel
        for SM_FAULT_ENABLE_OUTPUT and the same-day-added
        SM_FAULT_ENERPRO (both per-channel types, same as OVERCURRENT).
        Doc comments updated in both state_machine.h and .c (the "0xFF
        sentinel" framing, EnterFault()'s own comment) to describe all
        three per-channel types instead of just OVERCURRENT.

  Verify: Rebuilt clean for BOTH targets (controller +16 bytes, 90196;
        simulator 95192). Reflashed the SIMULATOR. NOT yet re-verified
        against a live ena_contact re-run -- that scenario's fault path
        (XrexIo_PollEnableOutputFaults() reading the CONTROLLER's own
        local ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT output pins) is entirely on the
        CONTROLLER side, so confirming the fix needs the controller
        reflashed too -- pending explicit go-ahead, per the standing
        rule (same flash this session is already waiting on for the
        Enerpro reclassification, below).

2026-09-18  —  SIM:LOG/SIM:LOGDATA? added -- onboard waveform logging
               for the simulator, replacing host-side polling
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  Direct correction: python/run_simulator_validation.py
        originally captured the simulator's DRIVE_HZ/FEEDBACK_HZ during
        a shot by polling SIM:CHANnel:STATus? at a fixed host-side
        interval (~10 samples/sec) -- coarse, and adds serial round-
        trip jitter to the very waveform being measured. User asked for
        the same "arm a log, run the shot, retrieve and plot
        afterward" pattern already established for the CONTROLLER side
        (pid.h's PID_ArmLog()/PID:LOGDATA?, used throughout this
        project's own bench sessions) instead.

        New sim_transrex.c waveform log: SimTransrex_ArmLog(channel,
        maxSamples, minIntervalMs)/GetLogCount()/GetLogSample() --
        SimTransrex_Update() itself appends a sample every real
        main-loop tick (no host polling at all), throttled by
        `minIntervalMs` rather than PID_ArmLog()'s tick-count decim
        (SimTransrex_Update() runs off the main loop, not a fixed
        hardware tick like PID_Update()'s HRTIM-driven 1kHz interrupt,
        so there's no fixed rate to decimate against) -- each sample
        instead carries its OWN elapsed-ms-since-armed timestamp.
        Single-channel-at-a-time, matching PID_ArmLog()'s own simpler
        (non-"All") shape. SIM_LOG_MAX_SAMPLES=1000 (sim_transrex.h) --
        an initial 2000 overflowed this MCU's RAM by 7200 bytes on a
        simulator build (on top of pid.c's own 48000-byte log arrays,
        pfm.c's 40000-byte table, everything else already resident),
        reduced to 1000 (matching PID_LOG_MAX_SAMPLES's own value),
        confirmed to link clean.

        New SIM:LOG <ch> <maxSamples> <minIntervalMs>/SIM:LOGDATA? <ch>
        commands (commands.c/.h, cmd_parser.c), same
        BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR-only guard as the rest of the SIM:
        namespace, same streaming-reply pattern as PID:LOGDATA?
        (cmd_pid_logdata()) to avoid needing one giant buffer.

        python/run_simulator_validation.py rewritten to arm both
        boards' logs (controller: PID:LOG, simulator: the new SIM:LOG)
        immediately before each scenario's shot and retrieve+plot both
        afterward, replacing every poll_sim_status() call site.

  Verify: Rebuilt clean for BOTH targets after fixing one syntax error
        (a doc comment containing a literal "XREX:CHANnel:*/PID:*"
        prematurely closed the enclosing /* */ block -- caught by the
        compiler, not silently wrong) and the RAM-overflow-driven size
        reduction above. Controller: 90180 bytes, byte-identical to the
        previous entry -- confirms this whole feature is simulator-only,
        zero controller footprint. Simulator: 95168 bytes. Reflashed
        the simulator, smoke-tested directly over serial (SIM:LOG 1 100
        10 -> SIM:LOGDATA? 1 correctly returned exactly 100 samples,
        10ms apart, driveHz=0/feedbackHz=5000 throughout -- an ungated
        channel, matching expectations). Then re-ran the baseline
        closed-loop scenario end-to-end through the rewritten
        orchestration script: 859 simulator samples + 1000 controller
        samples over a 3-second shot (vs. 29 samples with the old
        polling approach) -- both plotted cleanly, scenario still
        PASSED (final DRIVE_HZ=19992, target 20000).

2026-09-18  —  Enerpro fault reclassified out of General Fault
               (SM_FAULT_ENERPRO), PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A_PER_CHANNEL
               updated 5kA -> 6kA
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  User pointed at docs/Transrex/Transrex_Controls_Upgrade
        (1).pdf (the original 2026-07-10 system upgrade plan) as
        authoritative context while reviewing the Transrex simulator
        work. Its own fault table gives Enerpro faults the SAME
        response as Overcurrent (reduce surviving channels' DEMAND by
        25% x fault count, keep running) -- distinct from Water/Temp's
        full system stop. The firmware had all three (Water+Temp+
        Enerpro) routed to one SM_FAULT_GENERAL full-stop path, per an
        earlier direct instruction predating this document
        ("Water, Temperature, and Enerpro faults are considered
        general faults," 2026-09-17). Confirmed directly, 2026-09-18:
        reclassify Enerpro like OCP; Water+Temp unchanged.

        New SM_FAULT_ENERPRO fault type (state_machine.h), grouped with
        SM_FAULT_OVERCURRENT/SM_FAULT_ENABLE_OUTPUT in EnterFault()'s
        switch (state_machine.c) -- reuses HandleOvercurrentFault(channel)
        unchanged. New SM_ReportEnerproFault(channel), exact mirror of
        SM_ReportOcpFault()/SM_ReportEnableOutputFault()'s critical-
        section-protected shape.

        xrex_io.c: XrexIo_EvaluateGateDriverFault() no longer checks
        Enerpro's bit (Water+Temp only now). New
        XrexIo_PollEnerproFaults(uint16_t raw12) -- per-channel gated
        check of just the Enerpro bit, calling
        SM_ReportEnerproFault(channel) directly (matching
        XrexIo_PollOcpFaults()'s "evaluate and report" shape, not
        Water+Temp's "evaluate, let the caller latch/stop" shape --
        Enerpro's real response has no single shared "the fault" to
        hand back, each channel's is independent). gate_driver.c:
        GateDriver_CheckFault() now calls XrexIo_PollEnerproFaults() on
        every EXTI edge, on the SAME raw GateDriver_Read() already used
        for the (now Water+Temp-only) general-fault check -- no
        redundant GPIO read. commands.c: STATE? gained an `ENERPRO <ch>`
        branch alongside the existing OVERCURRENT/ENABLE_OUTPUT ones,
        same shape.

        Separately, same review: PFM_MAX_CURRENT_A_PER_CHANNEL
        (ctrlr_config.h) updated from the original 5000.0f placeholder
        (matching the document's own "5kA output LEMs" spec) to
        6000.0f -- confirmed directly: the REAL installed LEMs are 6kA,
        not 5kA. Still a shared default across all 4 channels, still
        flagged "MUST BE CALIBRATED BEFORE FINAL DEPLOYMENT" pending
        real per-channel measurement -- this is a better shared
        placeholder, not a measurement. PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ (5000 Hz =
        0 A) was checked against the same document and found already
        correct -- no change there.

  Verify: Rebuilt clean for BOTH targets (zero warnings either way).
        Controller: 90180 bytes (+200 from the previous entry -- this
        is real shared fault-routing logic, unlike the simulator-only
        sim_transrex.c work below, which the controller build strips

        NOT yet verified end-to-end (needs the controller reflashed too
        -- SIM:FAULT:ENERPRO only drives a transmit pin, it does not

        the new code to test for real): inject SIM:FAULT:ENERPRO on one

2026-09-18  —  sim_transrex.c/.h added: the Transrex simulator's actual
               closed-loop logic (DRIVE capture -> filter -> FEEDBACK,
               ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT gating, SIM:FAULT:/SIM:MODEL:/
               SIM:CHANnel:STATus? commands)
-----------------------------------------------------------------------
  Change:  First cut of Phases 1-5 from the approved simulator plan
        (/Users/everettpenne/.claude/plans/cuddly-nibbling-octopus.md),
        started while the physical fiber connectivity work (this same
        day's DIAGnostic:GPOut09/10 entry, below) was being wired up on
        the bench. Everything up to this point was raw pin
        connectivity -- this is what makes the simulator actually
        BEHAVE like a Transrex.

        SIMULATOR-ONLY module (BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR), new
        sim_transrex.c/.h:
          - Reuses pfm_input.c's existing continuous-capture mode
            (PfmInput_StartContinuous()/ConsumeAveragePeriod()),
            unchanged, on PFM_Input_01..04 to measure the controller's
            real commanded XRn_DRIVE frequency.
          - Single-pole low-pass filter (alpha = dt/(tau+dt), default
            tau=100ms, SIM:MODEL:TAU) applied directly in Hz -- no
            Amps-domain round-trip needed since AmpsToHz() (pid.c) is
            linear, per the plan's own documented assumption.
          - Writes the filtered result out via this board's own
            XRn_DRIVE HRTIM channels (HRTIM1_SetChannelPeriod(), same
            primitive pid.c uses), which the controller receives as its
            own XRn_FEEDBACK. Started UNCONDITIONALLY at boot
            (HRTIM1_PWM_Start() + per-channel output-enable), not tied
            to this board's own ARM/FIRE state -- a real Transrex
            responds because it's connected and enabled, not because it
            ran its own internal "shot."
          - Gated per channel on ENA_OUT+CONTACT_OUT both asserted,
            read from the controller via GateDriver_Read() (PE0..PE7)
            -- NOT XrexIo_Get*Output() (PG0..PG7), which are this
            board's OWN transmit pins for a different purpose (see
            below). Gated off: floors output to PFM_TURNON_FREQ_HZ
            (0A) rather than holding the last value -- decided per the
            plan's own flagged open question #2, on the reasoning that
            a real disconnected Transrex stops responding, it doesn't
            keep echoing.
          - Per-channel/per-category fault injection (Water+Temp
            combined -- SimTransrex_SetFaultWaterTemp(), one shared
            fiber+splitter per the finalized wiring; Enerpro; OCP),
            reusing XrexIo_SetEnableOutput()/SetContactorOutput() for
            Water+Temp/Enerpro (same physical PG0-7 pins XREX:CHANnel:
            ENAOut/CONTactOut already drive) and a small direct
            HAL_GPIO_WritePin() pair for OCP (PG8/PG9/PD0/PD1, no
            existing xrex_io.c setter for those). Default HEALTHY
            (HIGH, FAULT_POLARITY_NORMALLY_HIGH) at boot -- a
            DELIBERATE exception to this project's usual "every new
            output defaults LOW" convention, per the confirmed "default
            healthy" scope decision: the whole point of this module is
            to NOT present the floating/LOW-reads-as-fault state a
            disconnected real Transrex's pins show on the bench today.

        New SIM: command namespace (commands.c/.h, cmd_parser.c),
        guarded out of a controller build entirely (declarations,
        definitions, AND registration all share the same
        BUILD_TARGET_SIMULATOR guard -- there's no "simulated Transrex"
        concept on the real controller for this namespace to mean
        anything about): SIM:FAULT:WATERTEMP/ENERPRO/OCP <ch> <0|1>/?,
        SIM:MODEL:TAU <ms>/?, SIM:CHANnel:STATus? <ch> (DRIVE_HZ/
        FEEDBACK_HZ/ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT/the three fault states in one
        snapshot).

        main.c: SimTransrex_Init() called once at boot (after
        HRTIM1_FullInit()/PfmInput_Init(), both unconditional);
        SimTransrex_Update() called every main-loop iteration
        (alongside SM_PollFaults()/XrexIo_PollOcpFaults()) -- NOT from
        PID_Update(), which only ticks while THIS board's own HRTIM
        Master is running (i.e. only during a FIRE on this board,
        irrelevant here). New source file -- added to Debug/Core/Src/
        subdir.mk and Debug/objects.list by hand, same known gotcha as
        every prior new .c file this project (AGENTS.md).

        *** KNOWN LIMITATION, flagged directly in sim_transrex.h's own
        header comment: this build still runs the FULL controller
        firmware unconditionally, including its own state_machine.c/
        pid.c/gate_driver.c, all still watching the exact same PE0..PE7
        pins this module repurposes as ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT gating input
        (those pins are ALSO the simulator's own, unmodified XR1..4
        Water/Temp fault-detection inputs under gate_driver.c). Harmless
        as long as the simulator's own PID channels are never
        PID:CHANnel:ENAble'd on (gate_driver.c's fault evaluation is
        gated per-channel-enabled) -- differentiating main.c's own
        state-machine/PID polling to be controller-only, so this caveat
        goes away entirely, is a follow-up, not done here.

  Verify: Rebuilt clean for BOTH targets (zero warnings either way).
        Controller build: 89980 bytes, byte-identical to before this
        change -- confirms the new module and every SIM: command are
        fully stripped by --gc-sections on that target, zero footprint
        impact. Simulator build: 93712 bytes. Reflashed the simulator
        over the serial bootloader, confirmed live: *IDN? unchanged;
        SIM:MODEL:TAU?/TAU roundtrip (100 default, set/verified 250,
        reset back to 100); SIM:CHANnel:STATus? 1 returned
        `DRIVE_HZ=0 FEEDBACK_HZ=5000 ENA_OUT=LOW CONTACT_OUT=LOW
        WATERTEMP_FAULT=0 ENERPRO_FAULT=0 OCP_FAULT=0` -- correct
        gated-off floor behavior (no fibers driving ENA_OUT/CONTACT_OUT
        at test time) and correct default-healthy fault state;
        SIM:FAULT:OCP 1 1 -> re-query showed OCP_FAULT=1, then 1 0 ->
        back to 0 -- fault injection round-trips correctly.

        NOT yet verified: the actual closed-loop behavior with fibers
        connected and the controller actively driving/gated on (Phases
        1-3's real point) -- pending the physical fiber reconnection
        this same session's bench work was doing concurrently. Not
        committed to git yet.
